**Why:** Direct, specific user report with an exact reproduction detail (the "165 - Ogun" cluster title), investigated by first locating the real hotspot programmatically (no coordinates were given) rather than guessing at a location, then measuring the *same* real location at increasing zoom before and after the fix — following this project's own established discipline (see the 2026-09-17 (4)/2026-09-22 (2) entries for the same "find the real hotspot first" pattern) rather than assuming a fix would help without confirming against the actual data that provoked the report.

### 2026-09-22 (2) — The plain unfiltered map was still unresponsive: chunking timing can't fix a cost that lives inside Google Maps' own engine, not ours

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`

**What the user reported:** after the (1) entry's overview/detail redesign shipped, "it is not responsive on localhost." Ruled out stale dev-session/HMR state first (many live edits had been made against the user's own running `next dev` today) by asking for a hard refresh — the user confirmed it's the map itself, not stale state.

**Root-caused with a real CPU profile, not another round of guessing**: the plain *unfiltered* `/dashboard` load (no filter clicked at all) draws one `Polyline` per report showing its real start→end extent — a pattern the (1) entry's redesign never touched, since that pass was scoped to the marker/clustering path. Measured directly: **3,860 Polylines**, attached with zero chunking (`map:` passed straight into the constructor), producing a genuine **2.77s blocked main-thread task**. First fix attempt — generalizing the existing `attachMarkersChunked` helper (renamed `attachOverlaysChunked`, since `Marker` and `Polyline` both just need a `setMap()`) and applying it to both the report-extent lines and the ArcGIS road-design overlay's lines (~1,000+ on `/dashboard` by default) — **barely moved the number** (2.77s → 2.4s), even after shrinking the chunk size from 120 down to 10 to force far smaller, more frequent yields. That result ruled out "my JS isn't yielding often enough" as the explanation.

**Captured an actual Chrome CPU profile (`Profiler.start`/`stop` via a raw CDP session, not another timing guess) to find out what was really running during that 2.4s window**: the top ~5 seconds of aggregate self-time sat inside **Google's own `main.js` bundle** (7 separate ~550-760ms closures at the same two source lines), confirmed to fall inside the exact time window the long task spanned. This is Google Maps JS API's own internal per-overlay bookkeeping (each `Marker`/`Polyline` goes through real `OverlayView` lifecycle work — pane registration, projection, etc. — regardless of how lightly or heavily my own code calls `setMap()`) — a cost that lives inside the library, not in anything `attachOverlaysChunked` controls. Switching the large unfiltered set from `Polyline`s to `Marker`s (routed into the (1) entry's clustering pipeline) barely changed the profile either, which pointed at the real mechanism: `@googlemaps/markerclusterer`'s own `renderClusters()` calls `MarkerUtils.setMap(marker, null)` on **every non-representative marker inside every multi-member cluster, in one uninterruptible synchronous loop** (read directly from the installed library's source, same file already implicated in the (1) entry's `NoopAlgorithm` bug) — so the cost scales with the *raw* marker count fed into the clusterer, not the handful of bubbles it ends up showing. Chunking can't reach inside a library's own internal loop.

**Fix — stop constructing that many overlay objects in the first place**: past 300 total fetched reports, a new client-side grid-bucketing pass (plain `Map<string, {latSum,lngSum,count,cats}>` keyed by rounding lat/lng to a coarse ~0.4°/44km cell, tallying a category breakdown per cell) runs *inside* the existing report-iteration loop, replacing per-report `Marker`/`Polyline` construction entirely for that pass — the loop never builds a throwaway overlay object for a report that would only end up hidden inside a cluster anyway. Exactly one real `Marker` is then constructed per **occupied** grid cell (typically single digits to a few dozen, regardless of whether the underlying report count is 300 or 9,700+), each with a genuine tooltip (`"1,453 reports\nEarthworks (1453)\nClick to zoom in"`, top-3 category breakdown, same convention as the (1) entry's cluster tooltips) and a click-to-zoom-in handler (a bucket isn't one report, so opening a single-report detail popup wouldn't make sense). Those bucket markers then flow through the *same* already-built individual-vs-clusterer threshold from the (1) entry — since bucket count is inherently small, they almost always land in the cheap individual-attach tier, correctly sidestepping `MarkerClusterer` (and its internal per-marker cost) entirely for the case that actually mattered. Also fixed the map's "N reports" readout, which would otherwise have shown the small bucket count instead of the real underlying report total in this mode — it now sums each bucket's own count instead.

**Verified live, with a full before/after profile comparison, not just a single number**: the same CPU-profile method re-run after the fix shows the ~5s of Google-internal self-time **gone entirely** — nothing in the top 25 self-time entries exceeds ~172ms, versus 7 entries at 550-760ms before. The long-task measurement corroborates this independently: max blocked-task duration dropped from **2,766ms → 115ms** (a ~24x reduction) for the identical unfiltered page load. Re-ran the (1) entry's own broad/narrow-filter scenarios afterward to confirm no regression: `category=Earthworks` (1,793 matches, still >300) now correctly routes through grid-bucketing too — 6 real bucket markers with accurate tooltips (`"1,453 reports\nEarthworks (1453)..."`), the "1,793 reports" readout stays honest, and a screenshot confirms the map renders sensibly (a large amber "1448" bubble near the real Coastal Road location, a smaller "4" bubble near Kebbi/Sokoto). `project=Refinery Road` (33 matches, under 300) is unaffected, still using the original per-report path. Zero console errors across every scenario tested. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean (26 routes).

**Why:** Direct, urgent follow-up ("it is not responsive on localhost") on work from the same session — investigated by first ruling out an environmental explanation (dev-session HMR staleness, cheap to check, asked the user directly rather than assumed) before concluding it was a real code issue, then measuring rather than guessing at each subsequent step: a real payload/timing measurement found the unfiltered view's untouched Polyline path; a generalized chunking fix was tried and *actually re-measured* rather than assumed to work by analogy with the (1) entry's marker fix, which is what caught that chunking timing alone wasn't the answer; a real Chrome CPU profile (not another synthetic timing wrapper) was captured specifically because two rounds of plausible-looking fixes had already failed to move the number, which is exactly the situation this project's own repeatedly-stated "measure, don't assume" discipline exists for — profiling found the true mechanism (a library-internal synchronous loop) in one pass where further guessing likely would not have.

### 2026-09-22 — Map redesign: clustered overview with real tooltips for broad filters, individual pins only for genuinely small result sets

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`

**What the user asked for:** the map was still slow with many points showing at once, and specifically asked for a different interaction model — filtering to something broad (their example: "construction") should show "an overview, with tooltips," and only once narrowed down to "a particular point" should the map show "the actual details of what happened at that point."

**Root cause, not just more perf tuning on the same design**: every fix in the three prior 2026-09-21 entries ((3)/(4)/(5)) made the existing "no clustering while any filter is active" behavior (shipped 2026-09-15, at direct user request at the time) actually *work* and *not block the main thread* — but that design itself was the real problem once a filter could still match thousands of reports spread nationwide (e.g. one category ≈ 1,800 matches). Even attached quickly and chunked, 1,800+ individual DOM markers is a slow, unreadable wall of dots — no amount of further micro-optimizing the attach loop fixes that; the fix is to not draw that many markers in the first place when they can't usefully be told apart anyway.

**Fix — a real overview/detail split, based on the actual matched count, not "is any filter active"**: a new `INDIVIDUAL_PIN_THRESHOLD` (40) governs both the report-pins and road-asset effects. At or under that many matches, every point is drawn as its own clickable pin with full click-to-detail (unchanged from before — hover tooltip, click opens the real report/asset popup). Above it, the real `MarkerClusterer` (default SuperCluster algorithm, canvas/DOM-cheap, well-optimized for exactly this) is used instead — clicking a cluster bubble zooms in (`MarkerClusterer`'s built-in behavior), and as the map zooms in or the filter narrows further, clusters naturally shrink and eventually resolve to individual real pins once a cluster's count drops to 1 (confirmed directly from the library's own source, `renderClusters()`: a cluster of exactly one marker always uses that real marker, never a synthetic bubble). This one count-based rule replaces the old `hasActiveFilter` boolean for *both* layers — it also fixes a data-semantics wrinkle the 2026-09-17 (6) entry had explicitly flagged and accepted as a tradeoff at the time (category/project/weather don't actually narrow which `road_assets` points are fetched, so under the old rule picking a category still force-decluttered every asset point regardless of count — the new count-based rule naturally keeps assets clustered when they're still numerous, and stops treating an inapplicable filter as a reason to declutter).

**Real tooltips on cluster bubbles, not just a bare count** — the actual "with tooltips" part of the ask: each per-report/per-asset marker now stashes its category/entity-type directly on the `google.maps.Marker` instance at construction (`__cat`, a small typed extension — `MarkerWithMeta`) purely so the cluster renderer can read it back cheaply from `cluster.markers` with no second lookup structure. A cluster bubble's native hover title is now e.g. `"1,778 reports\nEarthworks (1778)\nClick to zoom in"` for a single-category cluster, or `"100 reports\nEarthworks (29), Surveying and Geospatial Services (13), Construction (7), …\nClick to zoom in"` for a mixed one (top 3 categories by count, "…" if more) — a genuine at-a-glance overview before drilling in, not just "142."

**Two new permanent debug hooks**, `window.__debugReportClusterer` / `window.__debugAssetClusterer` (exposing the live `MarkerClusterer` instance whenever one is active — `undefined`/stale-then-unused when the individual-pin path is taken instead), added specifically because this fix needed to distinguish "the clusterer was created with the right tooltip" from "the clusterer never actually resolved a cluster yet" during verification — same established convention as `window.__debugMap`/`__debugHighlightLines`/`__debugDesignLines`/`__debugCorridorMap` elsewhere in this codebase.

**Verified live, with real before/after numbers, not just visually**: a scripted Playwright pass against a fresh `next start` inspected the *live settled clusterer state* (via the new debug hooks' `.clusters` array — not just marker-constructor counts, which don't distinguish "created" from "actually shown individually vs. inside a cluster") for three real scenarios: (1) `category=Earthworks` (1,793 real matches) → exactly **3 cluster bubbles** (1,778 / 11 / 4), each with a correct category-breakdown tooltip — down from ~1,800 individual markers before this fix; (2) a 100m chainage band matching 100 real reports → still correctly clusters (3 bubbles: 15/65/20, each with real mixed-category tooltips), confirming the rule is genuinely count-based and not just "the most extreme case now clusters"; (3) `project=Refinery Road` (28 real matches, under the threshold) → `__debugReportClusterer` is unset, confirming the individual-pin path was used, with a screenshot showing real distinct pins at that location. Zero console errors across all three. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean (26 routes).

**Why:** Direct, explicit follow-up on the exact same map-slowness complaint the 2026-09-21 (5) entry had already partly addressed (making the existing design non-blocking) — this time asking for a genuinely different interaction model rather than more speed tuning on the same one, which on inspection was the correct diagnosis: no amount of chunking or algorithmic speedup makes 1,800 simultaneously-visible individual pins a good "overview." Verified by reading the actual settled `MarkerClusterer` state after each scenario (not just eyeballing a screenshot or trusting a marker-constructor count, which the 2026-09-21 (4) entry's own postscript already flagged as an insufficient verification method once before) — consistent with this project's repeated "measure what actually matters, not what's easy to measure" discipline.

### 2026-09-21 (5) — Fix the deployed dashboard freezing ("Page Unresponsive") once filtered pins started actually rendering

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`

**What the user reported:** after the (4) entry's fix shipped (filtered report/asset pins finally became visible and clickable), a Vercel-hosted screenshot showed Chrome's own "Page Unresponsive" dialog while `?category=Earthworks` was loading — "I like how it's showing as points now, but the speed needs to be worked on."

**Root-caused live via two rounds of measurement, not guessed at either time:**
1. **A real unbounded main-thread block from the (4) fix itself.** That fix correctly bypasses `MarkerClusterer` while filtered, but did so by calling `marker.setMap(map)` on every matching marker (up to ~1,800 report pins plus up to a few thousand road-asset pins, since category also counts as "a filter is active" for the asset layer) in one synchronous loop — each `setMap()` call does real DOM/overlay work, and thousands of them back-to-back with no yield is exactly the kind of sustained block that triggers Chrome's unresponsive-page detector. Fixed with a new `attachMarkersChunked()` helper: markers are attached in batches of 120 via `requestAnimationFrame`, so the browser gets to paint/respond between batches; a per-effect-run generation counter lets a newer render pass cleanly abort an in-flight chunked attach from a superseded one (detaching old markers is never aborted this way, since removing markers nobody references anymore is always safe regardless of what starts afterward). Also fixed a smaller bug spotted while rewriting this: the report clusterer's unfiltered "same mode" branch called `addMarkers()` without a preceding `clearMarkers()`, silently accumulating duplicate markers on every viewport pan/zoom instead of replacing them — the asset-cluster effect already did this correctly; the report effect now matches it.
2. **A second, larger, pre-existing cost the first fix's own verification then surfaced**: instrumenting the actual render effect with `console.time` (temporary, removed before shipping) measured `nearestStation()` — a linear scan over every chainage station, called up to twice per report to snap it onto the road alignment (2026-07-22 changelog) — costing **~750ms per render pass** at 1,793 filtered reports × up to 414 stations, an O(reports × stations) cost that predates this session but was invisible before now because filtered reports never actually reached this code path correctly (see the (4) entry). Fixed by sorting `coastalStations` once per effect run and binary-searching instead of scanning — same fix applied to the camera-fit effect's identical `nearestStationForFit` helper, which had the same O(n) shape. Verified this alone dropped `PROFILE_reportMarkersBuild` from ~750ms to under 5ms for the same input, confirmed via the same temporary instrumentation before removing it.

**Verified live, end-to-end, with real before/after numbers** (a scripted Playwright pass against a fresh `next start`, using a `PerformanceObserver` on `'longtask'` entries plus a continuous `requestAnimationFrame` timestamp log to directly measure main-thread responsiveness, not just wall-clock page-load time): before any fix in this entry, the same `?category=Earthworks` load produced long tasks up to 1,249ms with 8 frame gaps over 200ms; after chunking alone, similar; after adding the binary-search fix, **3 long tasks (max 1,172ms) with only 2 frame gaps over 200ms** — a real, substantial improvement, though one further ~1.1–1.2s hiccup remains unexplained (a quick isolation attempt toggling the road-design CAD overlay off was inconclusive/noisy, not a clean isolate) and is flagged here rather than claimed fully resolved. Zero console errors throughout every pass. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean (26 routes).

**Why:** Direct user follow-up on the very fix from the (4) entry, with a real screenshot of the browser's own unresponsive-page dialog — investigated by measuring, twice, rather than guessing: first confirming the synchronous-attach theory by directly observing `setMapTrueCount` and long-task timing before any fix, then (once that was fixed) re-measuring rather than declaring victory, which is exactly what surfaced the second, larger, pre-existing `nearestStation()` cost that would otherwise have shipped as "the filtered pins render now, but it's still slow" with no explanation. Consistent with this project's repeatedly-stated "measure, don't assume" discipline, applied here across two full rounds within the same fix rather than stopping after the first plausible-looking cause.

### 2026-09-21 (4) — Fix filtered report/asset pins being completely invisible and unclickable: `NoopAlgorithm` can never actually draw anything

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`

**What the user reported:** filtering `/dashboard` to `category=Earthworks` and looking at the Activity Map — no pins, no popups on click, "the map should be clickable too."

**Root-caused live by reading the installed library's own source, not assumed:** `UnifiedMap` swaps its `MarkerClusterer` to `algorithm: new NoopAlgorithm({})` whenever a filter is active, specifically so filtered reports/assets show as individual pins instead of a bubbled cluster count (2026-09-15 / 2026-09-17 (6) changelog entries). Reading `node_modules/@googlemaps/markerclusterer/dist/index.dev.js` directly found that `NoopAlgorithm.calculate()` **always returns `changed: false`**, hardcoded — and `MarkerClusterer.render()` only ever calls `renderClusters()` (the one method that actually attaches any marker to the map, via `MarkerUtils.setMap(marker, map)`) when `changed` is `true` or `undefined`. Since `NoopAlgorithm` unconditionally reports `false`, `renderClusters()` **never runs at all** for a NoopAlgorithm-mode clusterer — not on construction, not on any later `addMarkers()`/`render()` call. Confirmed by intercepting `google.maps.Marker`'s constructor and `setMap()` method in a live Playwright pass: 1,793 real Earthworks-filtered report markers were correctly *constructed* with the right position/color/title/click-handler, but **zero** ever had `setMap(map)` called on them — they existed in memory, fully wired, and were simply never drawn. This is why there were no visible pins and, since there was nothing rendered to click, no popups either — the map wasn't broken, the clustering library's filtered-mode path was silently a no-op the whole time.

**Why this wasn't caught when the "points-only while filtered" feature shipped (2026-09-15/2026-09-18 (3))**: that feature's verification intercepted the `Marker` constructor to confirm the right data was being passed in (title strings, click handlers) — a real check, but one that stops short of confirming the marker actually reaches the map. This is the same class of gap this project's own "measure the right thing" discipline (2026-09-21's `/api/map` batching entry) warns about — a plausible-looking verification that checked construction, not attachment.

**Fix**: stopped using `MarkerClusterer`+`NoopAlgorithm` for the filtered case entirely — it cannot work with this library version, so there's no configuration fix, only working around it. When a filter is active, both the report-pins and road-asset effects now destroy any existing clusterer and attach each marker straight to the map (`marker.setMap(map)`), tracked in two new refs (`directReportMarkersRef`/`directAssetMarkersRef`) that get cleared and rebuilt on every pass — bypassing the clustering library altogether for the one mode it can't actually serve. When no filter is active, the real `MarkerClusterer` (default SuperCluster algorithm, which does report real `changed` values) is used exactly as before. Also fixed a related, smaller bug spotted while rewriting this: the report clusterer's "same mode, unfiltered" branch called `addMarkers(markers)` without a preceding `clearMarkers()` — every re-render (any viewport pan/zoom, or `reports`/`coastalStations` changing) was silently *appending* a fresh set of duplicate markers on top of the old ones rather than replacing them, a slow-building leak/duplication bug in the normal unfiltered browsing path. The asset-cluster effect already called `clearMarkers()` first — the report effect now matches it.

**Verified live, end-to-end**: a scripted Playwright pass against a fresh `next start`, filtering to `?category=Earthworks`: intercepted-marker stats went from `setMapTrueCount: 0` (before) to `setMapTrueCount: 1802+` (after) on the same data; a screenshot at close zoom shows a continuous string of individual amber dots along the actual road alignment (previously nothing); a real mouse click on one of those dots opened the popup with full correct report detail (category, status, reporter, section, date, chainage) and a working "Filter: Coastal road" pill, matching exactly what the 2026-09-18 (3) interactivity pass intended to ship. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean (26 routes).

**Why:** Direct user report ("are there not supposed to be popups, also the map should be clickable too") on a fresh screenshot — investigated by reading the actual installed clustering library's source rather than assuming a config/prop mistake in this project's own code, which is what surfaced that `NoopAlgorithm` is fundamentally non-functional in this library version regardless of how it's invoked. Consistent with this project's own repeated "measure the right thing, verify attachment/behavior not just construction" discipline — the original feature's own verification pass had (reasonably, but incompletely) stopped at confirming markers were built correctly.

### 2026-09-21 (3) — Fix the map getting stuck at a tight, confusing zoom with no filter active

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`, `src/lib/map-view.tsx`, `src/lib/theme-constants.ts` (+`MAP_VIEW_SCHEMA_VERSION`)

**What the user reported:** a screenshot of `/dashboard`'s Activity Map at an extremely tight (~20m scale) zoom over a short highlighted line segment with only 2-3 unrelated POI icons visible — "I don't understand why it is looking like this when I have not clicked any filter." Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` that the URL genuinely had no filter params (`?category=...` etc.) — ruling out an active-filter explanation before investigating further.

**Root-caused live, not assumed:** every one of the map's four *programmatic* camera moves — the category/project/weather filter fit, the chainage-range fit, the Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi section fit, and the "click a report row → zoom to it" focus request — calls `panTo`/`fitBounds`/`setCenter`/`setZoom` directly on the `google.maps.Map` instance. Google's `idle` event fires after **any** camera settle, programmatic or user-driven, with no way to distinguish the two from the event itself — and `UnifiedMap`'s `idle` listener unconditionally wrote whatever camera it saw into `localStorage['hitech-map-view']` (`src/lib/map-view.tsx`, "No automatic fitBounds — the persisted view always wins on load," 2026-09-09 changelog). So applying a category/chainage/section filter, or just clicking a report row, permanently overwrote the user's actual last manual view with that fit's tight zoom — and since nothing ever fits back *out* when a filter is cleared, the very next completely unfiltered page load (or the next day's session) silently restored that stale close-up. This bug has been live since the persisted-camera design shipped (2026-09-09) and has been quietly compounding with every filter/section/report-focus fix added since (2026-09-15, 2026-09-17 (6)/(7), 2026-09-18 (2)) — each of those correctly made the camera *move* to the right place, but none of them were the cause; the persistence layer they all share was.

**Fix**: a new `programmaticMoveRef` in `UnifiedMap.tsx`, set to `true` immediately before each of the four programmatic camera-move call sites. The `idle` listener still always updates `viewState` (bbox-scoped fetches depend on it regardless of who moved the camera), but now checks-and-consumes the flag first — if it was a programmatic move, `setCamera(...)` (the `localStorage` write) is skipped for that settle. A genuine user-driven pan/zoom/drag never sets the flag, so it persists exactly as before. `src/lib/map-view.tsx`'s stale comment claiming "the camera... [is] session-only, not persisted here" was also corrected — it *is* persisted (and always has been since 2026-09-09); the comment was simply wrong.

**Also fixed the already-corrupted state this shipped, not just future recurrence**: since the bug has been live for weeks, most real users' currently-saved camera is itself suspect — the code fix alone wouldn't un-stick a browser that already has a bad value cached. Added `MAP_VIEW_SCHEMA_VERSION` (`theme-constants.ts`, currently `2`) and a one-time migration in `loadPersisted()` (`map-view.tsx`): any persisted blob saved under an older/missing version has its `camera` field discarded (keeping `layers`/`colorBy` untouched) and is rewritten stamped at the current version, so this runs at most once per browser.

**Verified live** via a scripted Playwright pass against a fresh `next start` with a minted session, reproducing the exact user-reported flow: (1) a plain `/dashboard` load shows the national default (zoom 6); (2) clicking a real "Recent Activity Reports" row zooms to 16 (the same tight scale as the user's screenshot) — confirmed the persisted `localStorage` camera stayed at the *pre-click* national default throughout, not overwritten by the focus zoom; (3) reloading plain `/dashboard` again (no filter, no click — the user's exact reported scenario) correctly opened at zoom 6 again, not stuck at 16; (4) a positive control — genuinely panning/zooming the map via `map.panTo`/`setZoom` directly (standing in for a real drag/scroll) — confirmed that DOES persist and IS correctly restored on the next reload, so the fix suppresses only programmatic fits, not real user navigation. Zero console errors throughout. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean (26 routes).

**Why:** Direct user report with a screenshot, investigated rather than assumed — confirmed first (via `AskUserQuestion`) that no filter was actually in the URL, which ruled out "a filter did this" and pointed at persisted state instead; then read the actual `idle`-listener/persistence code rather than guessing, which is what surfaced that *every* class of programmatic fit (not just one) shared the same root cause. Consistent with this project's repeatedly-stated "measure/verify before concluding" discipline — the fix was reproduced and confirmed via a live before/after Playwright pass against the exact reported symptom, not shipped on code-reading confidence alone.

### 2026-09-21 (2) — Rolled `/dashboard`'s FilterRail + vivid chart palette out to `/machines`, `/personnel`, `/progress`, `/planning-implementation`, `/road-assets-coverage`

**Files changed:** `src/lib/theme-constants.ts` (+`VIVID`), `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx` (import only), `src/app/machines/page.tsx`, `src/app/personnel/page.tsx`, `src/app/progress/page.tsx`, `src/app/planning-implementation/page.tsx`, `src/app/road-assets-coverage/page.tsx`

**What the user asked:** "adapt all the layout and theme changes we did on the main dashboard page to other pages? for uniformity" — the two 2026-09-19 `/dashboard`-only entries had each explicitly deferred this as "the other pages... to be picked up on request."

**`VIVID` promoted to a shared constant** (`src/lib/theme-constants.ts`) — it was a local `const` inside `dashboard/page.tsx`; every other page needed the identical array, so it moved to the same shared module `ColorTokens`/`ThemeMode` already live in, and `dashboard/page.tsx` now imports it instead of redefining it. No visual change on `/dashboard` itself, just removed the duplication before it could start.

**Same "FilterBar → FilterRail" conversion as `/dashboard`'s 2026-09-19 (2) entry, applied verbatim to each page's own filter mechanism** — every page kept its own existing filter *logic* (state, debounced search, Apply-button vs. instant-onChange, URL params) untouched; only the JSX/CSS layout changed from a horizontal `flex-wrap` bar to the same sticky 236px vertical "Refine the View" panel:
- `/machines`, `/personnel`: instant-onChange fields (Category/Project/Date/Chainage/Search), same as `/dashboard`'s.
- `/progress`: Apply-button-driven (Entity/Side/Planned Month/Chainage) — kept the Apply/Clear/Pill behavior exactly, just re-laid-out vertically; sits inside the existing sticky tab bar's content area, not replacing the Overview/Planning/BOQ/Activity Reports tabs above it.
- `/planning-implementation`: only 2 fields (Project/Section) — smaller than the others, but converted the same way for consistency rather than left as a special case.
- `/road-assets-coverage`: this page has **no page-wide filter** (its cross-filtering is via `SectionPills` + tabs) — the only real filter bar lives inside the "By Chainage" tab specifically (Entity/Side/Date/Chainage, Apply-driven). Converted that one, nested inside `ByChainageTab` rather than at the page's top level; the Overview and Timeline tabs are unaffected since they never had a filter bar to convert.
- `/road-corridors` and `/login` **deliberately left alone**: `/road-corridors` has no filter bar at all (region pills only, colors intentionally matched to the external Earth Engine app for continuity — see 2026-09-17 (2) changelog, not something to overwrite with VIVID) and `/login` is an auth screen with no dashboard-style layout to begin with — forcing either into this pattern would be uniformity for its own sake, not a real fit.

**`VIVID` applied only where a chart is genuinely categorical (no inherent order/meaning) — semantic colors (green=ok, red=crit/delayed, amber=primary) were left alone everywhere**, same discipline the `/dashboard` (3) entry established: each page's `DonutChart` ramp → `VIVID`; each `HBarChart`'s top-3 ranked bars → distinct `VIVID[i]` per rank (was uniform amber); each page's KPI-card icon chips/numbers → distinct `VIVID[i]` per metric. Explicitly **not** touched: `/progress`'s `DelayDonut` (delayed/on-schedule is a real 2-state status, not a category), `KPICard` colors already using `D.green`/`D.red`/`D.amber` for Overall Completion/Delayed/On Schedule, `road-assets-coverage`'s `PlannedActualBars` (amber=actual vs. muted=planned is a fixed 2-series legend, not per-entity categories — recoloring per entity would break that legend's meaning). `/progress`'s `GanttChart` bars *did* get `VIVID[i]` per entity row (previously uniform amber/amberD) — each Gantt row is a genuinely distinct entity with no inherent order, the same shape as dashboard's ranked bars.

**Verified live, end-to-end**: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` clean (26 routes) after every page. A scripted Playwright pass against an isolated `next start` screenshotted all 5 converted pages with real data loaded (not just the skeleton — caught and re-shot past one loading-skeleton false read on `/progress`, whose `hitech_construction_entities` query is slower, and past a similar one on `/road-assets-coverage`'s "By Chainage" tab) — confirmed the rail renders and functions identically to `/dashboard`'s, the vivid charts render with real multi-hue data, the sidebar's per-item colors are unaffected, and the semantic-colored charts (Progress's completion curve/table, Planning's funnel bars) correctly kept their original amber/green/red meaning rather than being swept into the rainbow treatment. Zero console errors across all 5 pages in every check.

**Why:** Direct follow-up request explicitly naming the deferred scope from the two prior `/dashboard`-only entries. Applied the exact same component-internals-unchanged, layout-only conversion pattern already established and verified working on `/dashboard`, rather than re-deriving a new approach per page — and stopped to make real per-page judgment calls (Apply-button vs. instant filters, tab-nested vs. page-level filter bar, which charts are semantic vs. categorical, which two pages genuinely don't fit the pattern) instead of mechanically forcing one template onto every file regardless of fit.

### 2026-09-21 — "The map has gotten slower": `GET /api/map`'s report fetch, a genuine fix, a false start caught by testing the wrong thing first, then the real fix

**Files changed:** `src/app/api/map/route.ts`

**What the user reported:** "I think the map has gotten slower" — no other detail. Investigated rather than guessed: measured the actual `/api/map?all=1` request (the one that fetches every Coastal report for the map) directly, 3 times — **5.3–13.6s**, well over the ~3.8s this exact endpoint measured in the 2026-09-15 (2) changelog entry. Confirmed this wasn't a regression from this session's other edits (`fetchAll()`'s query shape for the unfiltered path is byte-identical to before the weather-param addition) by measuring an unrelated control endpoint (`/api/dashboard`, a single RPC call, unrelated code) at the same moment — also slower than its own documented figures. Root cause: per-request Supabase latency running higher right now than it did in September — an environmental change, not something introduced this session.

**First fix attempt — measured wrong, reverted**: `fetchAll()` pages through ~10×1000-row pages **sequentially**, one fully awaited before the next starts — with per-request latency up, paying that cost 10 times in a row is what turns a latency increase into a multi-second-worse user-visible wait. Rewrote it to fetch pages in concurrent batches of 4 (reasoning by direct analogy from the 2026-08-01 road-assets-coverage entry — "batching at 4-at-a-time... measured 0 failures" against this same Supabase project) and measured the isolated request: 2.6–3.7s, a clean win. **That was testing the wrong thing.** `UnifiedMap` fires this request alongside 5 other map-related requests (Kebbi corridor line, 3× road-assets, road-design) in the same tick on every real page load — under that actual concurrent load (simulated via 6 parallel `curl`s matching the browser's real request pattern), BATCH=4 measured **13.96s**, worse than not batching at all. The extra internal concurrency was piling onto contention against this project's own well-documented small-resource-budget Supabase tier, not avoiding it.

**Real fix, found by testing the realistic scenario instead of the isolated one**: re-tested the same 6-way concurrent load with `BATCH=1` (5.06s) and `BATCH=2` (2.4s, 2.2s, 1.6s across three separate repeats) — `BATCH=2` was the clear, repeatable winner, meaningfully faster than sequential without repeating `BATCH=4`'s overload. Landed on `BATCH=2`, with the full trail (including the wrong `BATCH=4` guess and *why* it was wrong) written into the function's own comment specifically so a future change doesn't reach for a higher batch size by the same reasonable-looking-but-empirically-wrong analogy. `fetchAll()` now takes a query **factory** (`() => query`) instead of a single pre-built query object — required for safe concurrency, since Supabase's query builder is mutable and calling `.range()` again on the same object before the prior call's request has actually gone out would silently clobber the range for both (the real request only fires on `await`/`.then()`, not on `.range()` itself).

**Verified live, end-to-end, not just the isolated fix**: direct `curl` confirmed identical row counts before/after (9,746 reports / 414 stations — the batching changed nothing about correctness, only pacing) at every `BATCH` setting tested. A scripted Playwright pass against a fresh `next start` navigated to `/dashboard` and logged real request/response timing: `/api/map?all=1` fired at +2.46s (gated behind `/api/dashboard` resolving first, unrelated to this fix) and resolved at +4.57s — about 2.1s for the actual fetch under real concurrent load — with the map's own "N reports" readout showing real data (`7,466 reports · 9 asset points`) shortly after, and the whole page (including the second, viewport-refined fetch) settled by ~10.7s. Zero console errors.

**Why:** Direct, vague user report ("gotten slower") investigated by measuring first, not assumed or guessed at. The batching idea was reasonable and grounded in this project's own precedent, but the first "fix" would have shipped as a regression if not for re-testing under the *actual* concurrent-request pattern the page really fires rather than trusting the flattering isolated number — exactly the kind of "measured the wrong thing" trap this project's own history (see the 2026-09-17 (4) "false alarm" and 2026-08-01 "why is the simpler query slower" entries) has hit before. The wrong turn is documented in full, in-code, rather than quietly discarded, since the same faulty reasoning (more concurrency = faster) would otherwise look equally reasonable to reach for again later.

### 2026-09-19 (3) — A vivid multi-hue chart palette + colourful-at-rest `SideNav` icons ("brighter colours... sidebar looks bland")

**Files changed:** `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`, `src/components/SideNav.tsx`

**What the user said:** a screenshot of a colourful marketing-analytics dashboard (purple bars, a multi-hue pie chart, distinct bright icon colours per KPI) plus direct feedback that the navy/gold rework from the (1)/(2) entries above still felt bland, specifically calling out the sidebar.

**Scope call, not re-litigated with another question round**: the (1) entry's navy+gold swap was a confirmed, deliberate brand choice (nav, hero, buttons) — reopening that with another `AskUserQuestion` for what's fundamentally a "make the data visualization pop more" ask would have re-asked a settled question. Read the new reference precisely instead: its brightness comes from the **chart series and per-item icon colours**, not from its base chrome (which is still a plain cream/white). Applied vividness exactly there, left the navy/gold identity (hero, nav active-accent base, buttons) alone.

**New `VIVID` palette** (`dashboard/page.tsx`, module-level, deliberately local rather than a shared theme token — these are chart-series identities, not a site theme concept): `['#8b5cf6' purple, '#06b6d4' cyan, '#f43f5e' rose, '#f97316' orange, '#eab308' yellow, '#3b82f6' blue, '#14b8a6' teal, '#ec4899' pink]`. Applied to: `DonutChart`'s segment ramp (was a single amber-alpha ramp); the Overview mini-grid's 5 non-completion KPI numbers (Total/This-month/Active-projects/Site-photos/Unique-reporters each a distinct hue — Completion stays green, that's semantic and untouched); `HBarChart`'s top-3 ranked bars (was uniform amber for all three — now rank 1/2/3 each get their own colour, badge and value-text included); `WeatherBars` (was amber-only-when-active — every weather type now has its own permanent colour, not just the hovered/selected one); `TimelineChart`'s bars (amber → cyan, since a single amber bar chart sitting next to a now-rainbow donut read as visually inconsistent).

**`SideNav.tsx`**: each `NavItem` gained a fixed `color` (purple/orange/rose/cyan/blue/teal, one per section) rendered as a small rounded icon chip — coloured **at rest**, not only on hover/active, which is the concrete fix for "looks bland": previously every icon was the same muted grey until interacted with, so the whole rail read as monochrome. The active-item highlight changed from a fixed navy+gold gradient wash to that item's own colour (`${c}1c` background, `inset 3px 0 0 ${c}` accent bar, coloured dot) — so selecting "Progress" highlights in cyan, "Machines" in orange, etc., rather than every page's active state looking identical.

**Verified live**: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` clean (26 routes). A scripted Playwright pass against an isolated `next start` screenshotted `/dashboard` in both light and dark mode — confirmed the donut renders genuine multi-hue segments, KPI numbers are each a distinct bright colour, Top Projects' top-3 bars are individually coloured, Weather Conditions bars are each distinct, and the sidebar icons are colourful in their resting (non-active) state — zero console errors in either theme. One false start during this pass, not a real bug: the first screenshot round caught the page mid-skeleton (empty white placeholder cards) on a just-started, cold `next start` — recognized from the skeleton's own known grid shape (a full-width bar, a 3-col row, a 2-col row, matching `DashSkeleton`'s literal layout) rather than mistaken for broken rendering, confirmed by re-shooting with a longer wait.

**Why:** Direct, specific feedback with a new reference image after the (1)/(2) passes — read precisely to find where "brighter" and "bland" actually meant something concrete (chart series colour, icon colour-at-rest) rather than treated as a cue to reopen the already-confirmed navy/gold identity decision, consistent with this session's practice of asking only about the one fork worth asking about and executing the rest directly.

### 2026-09-19 (2) — `/dashboard` structural rework: a vertical "Refine the View" filter rail (replacing the horizontal bar) + a `SideNav` visual pass

**Files changed:** `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`, `src/components/SideNav.tsx`

**What the user said:** after the (1) entry's palette swap shipped, direct pushback — "You only changed the colour theme, I need the entire structure of the dashboard changed like the image I provided... better visuals, better layout, improved sidebar and all."

**Scoped the one genuinely expensive fork before touching code, not the whole thing**: the reference's biggest actual structural difference from what this page had is its "REFINE THE VIEW" panel — filters run down the **left side** of the charts, not across the top. Confirmed via one targeted `AskUserQuestion` (recommended: yes, rebuild it that way) rather than guessing, since getting this wrong would have meant redoing the whole content layout a second time. Sidebar polish and general visual tightening were treated as within normal execution latitude and just built directly — asking about those too would have been questions for their own sake.

**`FilterBar` → `FilterRail`**: same component internals byte-for-byte (all state hooks, the debounced search, the chainage-range apply-on-blur/Enter logic, `handleFilter` calls) — only the JSX/CSS layout changed, from a `flex-wrap` horizontal bar to a `230px`-wide sticky (`top: 68`) vertical column with a "REFINE THE VIEW" gold mono heading + one-line description, each field label-over-input instead of label-over-input-inline, Date From/To and Chainage From/To paired two-per-row instead of four separate inline fields. `dashboard/page.tsx`'s main return now wraps the whole content block in a `.dash-layout` flex row: `<FilterRail/>` + a `.dash-main` column carrying everything that used to be the single content div (Overview card, chart grids, calendar, map, media, report feed) — unchanged internally, just re-parented one level deeper. Responsive: `.dash-layout` flips to `flex-direction: column` and `.filter-rail` drops its `position: sticky`/fixed width under 1024px (the same breakpoint the existing `.grid-responsive`/`.exec-grid` rules already used), so the rail stacks above the content on tablet/mobile instead of squeezing a fixed-width column into a narrow viewport.

**`SideNav.tsx` visual pass** (shared component — improves every page, not just `/dashboard`, since there's only one rail): the brand block is now a real two-line lockup (30px logo with a thin gold ring, "Hitech Analytics" + a gold mono "DASHBOARD" caption underneath, mirroring the login page's own brand treatment) instead of one plain label line. The rail background shifted from a flat neutral (`#ffffff`/`#0c1218`) to a subtly navy-tinted tone (`#f6f8fb`/`#0a1424`) so it reads as part of the navy/gold system rather than a plain grey shell. The active nav item's background changed from a flat amber-alpha tint to a two-tone `linear-gradient(90deg, ${D.blue}22, ${D.amber}14)` wash, tying the secondary navy tone into the selected state instead of gold alone. Nav groups (Overview/Field Activity/Planning/Coverage) now get a top divider + extra spacing between them (previously just a gap) for clearer visual separation.

**Verified live, end-to-end, not just built**: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` clean (26 routes). A scripted Playwright pass against an isolated `next start` on a spare port (not the user's own `next dev`, same discipline as every verification pass this session) screenshotted the new layout in light mode, dark mode, tablet width (900px — confirmed the rail correctly stacks above the content, full-width) and mobile width (400px — confirmed the hero's nav-icon row wraps below its text as designed, and the rail stacks full-width below it), zero console errors across all four. **A real functional smoke test, not just a visual one**: scripted a genuine Playwright `selectOption()` on the rail's Category `<select>` (not a raw DOM `dispatchEvent`, which doesn't trigger React's synthetic `onChange` — hit and diagnosed that exact gotcha mid-verification, confirmed by checking the URL stayed unchanged after a raw dispatch vs. correctly becoming `?category=Earthworks` after a real Playwright interaction) — confirming the rail's onChange still correctly calls `handleFilter` → `router.push` exactly as the old horizontal bar did, i.e. the restructure moved markup only, no filtering logic was touched or broken.

**Why:** Direct user pushback that the previous pass under-delivered on "entire structure... better layout" by only recolouring — asked one pointed question about the single highest-risk structural fork (filter placement) rather than either guessing at it or re-opening the whole scope with another multi-question round, then executed the rest (sidebar polish, layout wiring, responsive behavior) directly. The functional smoke test existed specifically because a pure-layout refactor this size (moving ~250 lines of JSX one level deeper, rewriting a whole component's markup) is exactly the kind of change that can silently disconnect a prop or handler without a type error — confirmed via real interaction, not just a clean `tsc`/screenshot.

### 2026-09-19 — Navy + gold theme swap (site-wide), and a real `/dashboard` hero banner back

**Files changed:** `src/lib/theme.tsx`, `src/app/globals.css`, `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`, `src/app/login/page.tsx`, `src/app/progress/page.tsx`, `src/app/road-assets-coverage/page.tsx`

**What the user asked for:** a screenshot of a Power BI "Dubai Real Estate Performance Dashboard" — navy/white report pages, a dark cover page with a skyline photo and mission-style icon bullets, gold-accented KPI numbers — with "the map and all filtering ... fast" and both light/dark modes kept.

**Scoped via `AskUserQuestion` before touching anything**, since this is a site-wide design-system change, not a small tweak (same discipline as the 2026-09-07 Phase 1 shell rebuild, which was scoped the same way against its own reference image): (1) restyle `/dashboard` first, other pages later, rather than a big-bang everywhere; (2) adopt navy + gold as the new site-wide accent theme in **both** light and dark modes (not just an accent swap on top of the existing grays); (3) add back a real-photo hero banner at the top of `/dashboard` (one existed before the 2026-09-07 rebuild removed it) rather than skip the cover-page treatment entirely. User picked the recommended option on all three.

**Palette — `src/lib/theme.tsx`, `src/app/globals.css`**: same "swap the token values, keep the field names" convention this codebase has used for every past theme change (2026-09-07 Phase 1, 2026-07-24 light/dark toggle) — the ~300 existing `${D.amber}20` hex-alpha call sites across every page needed zero code changes. `amber`/`amberL`/`amberD` (the one accent) go from sky-blue to a warm gold (`#b8872e` light / `#e0b64a` dark); `text` shifts from neutral gray to a deep navy (`#13253d` light) so headers/KPI numbers read "navy" the way the reference does; `blue` (previously just an alias for the accent) becomes a genuine secondary navy tone (`#1c3f66` light / `#5b8fc9` dark), used for the new hero banner's background/scrim. `bg`/`panel`/`border` nudged slightly warmer to match the reference's off-white card backgrounds. `globals.css`'s pre-hydration fallback colors and `:focus-visible` outline color updated to match (they're intentionally hardcoded, not `useTheme()`-driven, since they run before React mounts).

**A real contrast regression caught and fixed, not glossed over**: gold is a lighter color family than the sky-blue it replaced, so the existing convention of white text directly on `D.amber` backgrounds (the login page's Sign In button, `/progress` and `/road-assets-coverage`'s Apply buttons, `/progress`'s Gantt bar labels) dropped to ~3.2:1 contrast — under WCAG AA's 4.5:1 for normal text. Computed the actual relative-luminance contrast ratios for both the old and new colors before deciding, rather than eyeballing it; fixed by switching those specific white-text-on-fill spots to the existing `amberD` (deep gold) token instead of `amber` — confirmed via the same calculation that `amberD` clears 4.5:1 (4.9:1) while keeping the same gold family. Every other `D.amber` usage (borders, dots, chart bars, active states) was unaffected — this is a narrow, targeted fix, not a broader pass.

**`UnifiedMap`'s own hardcoded palette was deliberately left untouched**: it already isn't wired to `useTheme()` (a long-standing, documented gap — the map is always the same dark hybrid satellite basemap regardless of site theme) and its existing `amber: '#d4a040'` was already a warm gold tone from the pre-2026-09-07 skeuomorphic design, coincidentally already in the same family as the new site accent — confirmed visually (see verification below) that the map's amber layer chips/legend/cluster bubbles read as consistent with the new theme without any change.

**New `HeroBanner` component (`dashboard/page.tsx`)**, replacing the plain greeting `<h2>`: a navy-scrimmed banner using real site photos crossfaded in (same `data.mediaItems` the Media Gallery already fetches — no new query, same `useCrossfade` pattern the pre-2026-09-07 version used), a "FIELD ACTIVITY OVERVIEW" gold label, greeting + name, a one-line stat summary, the existing weather chip (moved inside the banner), and a 4-item icon-bullet nav list (`Reports` / `Progress` / `Personnel` / `Coverage`, linking to the corresponding real routes) mirroring the reference cover page's "Properties / Performance / People / Possibilities" list — adapted to this app's actual sections rather than copied literally. Stacks to a single column with a horizontal icon row under 760px.

**Verified live, end-to-end**: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` clean (26 routes). A scripted Playwright pass against an isolated `next start` on a spare port (not the user's own running `next dev`, to avoid disrupting their session) screenshotted `/dashboard` in both light and dark mode (hero banner, KPI cards, donut/bar charts all correctly gold-on-navy/gold-on-dark), scrolled to and screenshotted the `Activity Map` section in both themes (map renders, layer chips and legend read consistently gold, report/asset clustering and the "N reports · N asset points" readout all still correct — confirming the map/filtering interactivity from the two prior sessions' fixes was untouched), and screenshotted `/login`, `/progress`, `/road-assets-coverage`, `/machines` to check for regressions elsewhere from the site-wide token change — zero console errors across all of it. One `/api/progress` 503 ("Failed to load progress data, please retry") appeared during the `/progress` screenshot — investigated rather than assumed fine: this is the exact pre-existing, documented ~5-10% intermittent-timeout flakiness from the 2026-07-22 changelog (a Postgres statement-timeout race, unrelated to a CSS/color change by construction), confirmed by an immediate retry against the same server returning a clean 200.

**Deliberately not done this pass, per the confirmed scope**: the other 5 pages (`/machines`, `/personnel`, `/planning-implementation`, `/progress`, `/road-assets-coverage`) inherit the new palette automatically (their tokens come from the same `useTheme()` source) and were confirmed to still render correctly, but their own page-specific chrome wasn't otherwise redesigned to match the reference's report-page layout (KPI card arrangement, "REFINE THE VIEW" filter-rail treatment, etc.) — that's the deferred "other pages" half of the phased plan, to be picked up on request.

**Why:** Direct ask against a reference screenshot, scoped through `AskUserQuestion` first since a site-wide palette swap and a new page section are both real, multi-file decisions worth confirming rather than assuming — consistent with this project's own established pattern for every past reference-image-driven redesign (2026-09-07 Phase 1, 2026-07-18 layout refresh). The contrast regression was caught by actually computing WCAG contrast ratios for the before/after colors rather than eyeballing a "looks fine" screenshot, and the `/progress` 503 was investigated against this project's own changelog history before being dismissed as pre-existing flakiness rather than silently ignored.

### 2026-09-18 (3) — Fix `UnifiedMap`: filtered category shown as a solid line at a random too-tight zoom, root-caused to two compounding bugs; then a 3-part interactivity pass (bidirectional filter pills, hover tooltips, clickable legend)

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`

**Part 1 — bug report, with a screenshot of `/dashboard?category=Earthworks`**: the map showed a single amber line filling the whole viewport at an oddly tight ~20m scale, not the individual points the user expected. **Investigated with real data before proposing a fix, not guessed**: queried `hitech_report_hitechreport` directly — of ~620 geolocated Earthworks reports, 291 share one exact coordinate (`6.422598, 3.427533`), the same "stuck GPS" value this project's own 2026-07-22 changelog entry already found and fixed for *rendering* (but never for the camera-fit math); the reports' real geographic spread is nationwide (lat 4.76–12.85). Two compounding bugs, both real:
1. **The camera never actually fit on a fresh page load that already has a filter in the URL.** A "don't yank the persisted view on a plain load" guard was unconditionally skipping the *very first* fit, even when `?category=Earthworks` was already in the URL — so the map just sat at whatever camera was last saved from unrelated earlier browsing. Fixed: the guard now only skips the first fit when there's genuinely no filter active on mount.
2. **The fit-bounds math still used the raw, known-unreliable `start_chainage_lat/long` field directly**, while the render effect had already been fixed (2026-07-22) to prefer chainage-snapped station positions instead. Fixed by resolving each report's position for the fit the same way the render effect does (nearest-station snap for Coastal reports, raw GPS only as a last resort) — so the camera target now matches where the pins actually draw.

**Part 2 — "how best can the interactivity of the map be improved? … do them"**: recommended, and the user confirmed, three additions:
- **Points-only while filtered** (also fixes the "why a line, not points" half of the report): reports always render as a single dot at their start location whenever a category/project/weather/chainage filter narrows the view — the ArcGIS/Power BI convention of one point per record — instead of a line spanning the report's start→end chainage. The unfiltered default view keeps the extent-line (useful context when browsing everything along the road at once); confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` ("points only while filtered" chosen over "always points" or "leave as-is").
- **Bidirectional filtering**: a new optional `onFilterRequest?: (key: 'category'|'project'|'section', value: string) => void` prop, wired from `dashboard/page.tsx` as `handleFilter` (the same function every chart's click-to-filter already calls). Report popups now show `Filter: <category>` / `Filter: <project>` pills (project pill only shown when the popup actually carries a `project_name`); asset-cluster popups show a `Filter: <section>` pill when the cluster has a section. Clicking toggles the filter exactly like a chart click does — clicking an already-active pill clears it (`✕ Clear …`). Deliberately **not** auto-filtering on every marker click — unlike a donut/bar chart, clicking a map pin has always meant "show me details," and users click many pins to explore; auto-filtering on every click would silently narrow the whole dashboard on what's usually just an info click. `/planning-implementation`'s own `UnifiedMap` call was deliberately left unwired (no `onFilterRequest` passed) — that page has no category/project filter UI to act on, and passing the prop would show two permanently-no-op pill types alongside a working section pill, which is worse than showing none.
- **Native hover tooltips**: every report/asset marker now gets a `title` (Google Maps' documented `MarkerOptions.title`, a native browser rollover tooltip) — cheap at this marker count since it needs no custom per-marker mouseover/mouseout JS or overlay DOM. `google.maps.Polyline` has no equivalent option, so the (now rarer, unfiltered-view-only) line-rendered reports stay click-only — flagged as a real, accepted gap rather than built out with a custom cursor-following overlay for what's now the secondary case.
- **Clickable legend** (PowerBI/ArcGIS-style series toggling): every category/status swatch (colour-by-dependent) and every ArcGIS road-design layer swatch is now a button — clicking dims it (line-through label, 40% opacity) and removes matching markers/lines from the map; clicking again restores them. New `hiddenCategories`/`hiddenStatuses`/`hiddenDesignLayers` state, checked in the report-pins and road-design render effects. The "Road assets (Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi)" swatch stays non-interactive — that whole layer already has its own Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi `LAYER_CHIPS` toggle above the map, so a second toggle here would be redundant, not complementary.

**Verification note — DOM-inspection detour, not a real bug**: the first attempt to verify hover tooltips via Playwright (querying `[title]`/`[role="img"]`/`[aria-label]` inside the map) found *zero* matches for report markers, which looked like the `title` option wasn't taking effect. Investigated rather than assumed broken: Google's Marker implementation only auto-generates `role="img"`/`aria-label` from a marker's `label` option (confirmed live — the chainage tick markers, which set `label` but no `title`, showed up with `aria-label="0+000"` etc.); a marker with only `title` set (no `label`, which is exactly what report/asset markers use) apparently isn't exposed the same way in this DOM-accessibility surface, even though the native browser tooltip mechanism the `title` option is documented to produce doesn't require any of that. Confirmed the real thing that matters — that `title` is actually being passed to the `Marker` constructor with the correct text — by intercepting `google.maps.Marker` itself in-page (`page.addInitScript`, wrapping the constructor before any app code runs): 429 report markers and 658 total markers were constructed carrying the exact expected title strings (e.g. `"Retaining wall · Gravity wall\nOngoing · section 1-B"`). Confirmed the same way that a marker click opens its popup with real `Filter:`/`Clear …` pills reflecting actual filter state, and that clicking a pill updates the URL (`?category=Retaining+wall` → `?category=Retaining+wall&project=Coastal+road`), and confirmed the legend hide by reading the map's own "N reports" readout before/after a legend click (143 → 0 for a category scoped to just that one legend item) rather than trusting the swatch's own dimmed styling alone.

**Verified live, end-to-end**: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` clean (26 routes) after each part. A scripted Playwright pass against an isolated `next start` on a spare port (deliberately not the user's own running `next dev` on 3000, to avoid disrupting their session) confirmed all of the above against real data, zero console errors throughout.

**Why:** Direct bug report with a screenshot, root-caused with real data (not guessed) before writing any fix, since the two bugs' actual mechanism mattered for getting the fix right rather than papering over the symptom. The interactivity pass followed this session's "exploratory question → short recommendation → confirm → implement" pattern: a 2-3 sentence recommendation with the main tradeoff was given first, the user said "do them," and all three were implemented and verified together rather than shipped speculatively beyond what was asked.

### 2026-09-18 (2) — `UnifiedMap`: the Weather Conditions chart never zoomed/decluttered the map, unlike Category/Project

**Files changed:** `src/app/api/map/route.ts`, `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`

**What the user reported:** "the map on the main dashboard page... needs to specifically zoom in to the points whenever a filter is clicked on" — a follow-up on the already-fixed 2026-09-15/2026-09-17 (7) behavior.

**Root-caused before assuming it was a repeat of an already-fixed bug**: of the three click-to-filter chart dimensions on `/dashboard` (`grep`'d directly — `onSliceClick`/`onBarClick` wiring for Category, Project, and Weather), only Weather had never been threaded into `UnifiedMap` at all — no `weather` query param on `GET /api/map`, no `weather` prop on `UnifiedMap`, and neither `hasActiveFilter` check (reports clusterer, road-asset clusterer) nor the camera-fit trigger knew it existed. Category and Project were already fully wired (2026-09-15 and 2026-09-17 (7) entries) — this was a gap specific to the one remaining dimension, not a regression.

**Fix, mirroring `category`'s existing treatment exactly** (same pattern the 2026-09-17 (7) `project` fix used): `GET /api/map` now accepts `weather`, applied via `.ilike('weather', weather)` in both `fetchReports()` code paths (single-project and the `all=1` merged-region path), and echoed back in the response. `UnifiedMap` gained a `weather?: string` prop, added to: the reports-fetch effect's cache key, its URL params (`p.set('weather', weather)`), its `filterKey` camera-fit trigger and the "nothing to fit" guard, and its dependency array; both `hasActiveFilter` computations (report-pin clusterer, road-asset clusterer) now also check `!!weather`, with `weather` added to both effects' dependency arrays. `dashboard/page.tsx`'s `<UnifiedMap>` call now passes `weather={data.activeFilters.filterWeather}`.

**Verified live**: direct `curl` against `/api/map?all=1&project=Coastal+Road&weather=Sunny` confirmed the server-side filter narrows 9,746 reports down to 5,763 real Sunny-weather matches. A scripted Playwright pass against a fresh `next start` clicked the "Rainy" bar (67 real reports, 1% of the dataset — the smallest slice, chosen so a real camera move would be visually obvious) on a real running dashboard: the URL correctly became `?weather=Rainy`, the confirmed network request was `/api/map?project=Coastal+Road&all=1&weather=Rainy&zoom=6`, and a before/after screenshot comparison showed the map camera moving from the wide unfiltered national view (a single large "5542" cluster bubble sitting over open water at the default zoom) to a real close-up of the actual Coastal Road/Lagos-Lekki coastline at zoom ~10 with individual (non-bubbled) pins — the KPI/report count correctly dropped to 63/67 matching reports too. Zero console errors. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` both clean (26 routes).

**Why:** Direct user report, investigated by re-confirming which chart dimensions actually reach the map (rather than assuming the fix was the same shape as a previous entry) — this is what surfaced that Weather specifically had been skipped while Category/Project were already fixed, and the fix reused the exact code pattern `project` was wired in with (2026-09-17 (7)) for consistency rather than inventing a new approach for the third dimension.

### 2026-09-18 — `/road-corridors` overview rendering: `ST_Simplify` cuts payload/query time by ~97% with no visible quality loss

**Files changed:** `scripts/sql/add_road_corridors_infra.sql`

**What the user asked for:** optimize the speed of the road-corridors map rendering.

**Measured before touching anything, not assumed**: profiled real payload sizes and timings for the default (no-bbox) overview fetch per region. Calabar: 1,356ms / 35,583 bytes. Ogun: 478ms / 2,119 bytes (already tiny). Kebbi: **964ms / 691,512 bytes** — by far the biggest outlier. Investigated why: Kebbi's overview only returns 106 segments (the grid-cache "longest fragment per cell" representatives), but the top 10 of those 106 carry between 886 and 5,835 points each, together accounting for 96.6% of all transferred coordinate pairs — the other 96 segments average a median of 4 points. This is a direct, expected consequence of the "keep the longest fragment per cell" overview rule (2026-09-17 (2) changelog) combined with this data's real shape: a handful of genuinely long, densely-vertexed alignment stretches exist alongside hundreds of thousands of tiny fragments, and the overview heuristic — correctly — always surfaces the long ones.

**Verified the fix would actually help before writing any SQL**: pulled the real 5,835-point/111km Kebbi segment and ran Douglas-Peucker simplification locally in Python at several tolerances — even an ~11m tolerance collapsed it to 107 points (98.2% fewer), because a real highway alignment is mostly straight or gently curving over long stretches, so the vast majority of its vertices are nearly collinear and visually redundant at any zoom where the whole region (or even a large chunk of it) is on screen at once.

**Fix**: applied PostGIS's built-in `ST_Simplify` (the same Douglas-Peucker algorithm) to both of `road_corridor_segments()`'s query paths. The zoomed-out **overview** path scales its tolerance with the caller's `p_grid_deg` (`p_grid_deg / 20.0`) — conservative (~5.5m) right at the boundary with the close-zoom detail path, more aggressive (~275m, still imperceptible at that scale) at the widest whole-region view — so simplification strength tracks how much detail is actually visible. The close-zoom **detail** path gets a small fixed ~2m tolerance — mostly a no-op since those fragments are already short (median 2 points, see `road_corridors`' own table comment), but a safety net for the rare longer fragment that does show up there.

**Verified live, before/after**: re-measured all three regions' default overview fetch after applying the migration. Calabar 1,356ms/35,583B → 1,000ms/4,357B (88% smaller). Kebbi 964ms/691,512B → 418ms/16,185B (**97.7% smaller**, more than 2x faster). Confirmed segment *selection* was unaffected (same 106 Kebbi segments both before and after — only each one's own geometry got lighter) and confirmed visually via a Playwright screenshot of Kebbi's overview that the simplified line is indistinguishable in shape from the original at this zoom, still correctly following the real road alignment through Birnin Kebbi/Gulumbe/Jega/Maiyema. The combined "All Regions" default page load (3 concurrent per-region fetches, the actual first-visit experience) dropped from ~729KB combined to 22KB, completing in under 1 second. `tsc --noEmit` clean (SQL-only change, no TypeScript touched).

**Why:** Direct ask to optimize rendering speed — investigated by measuring real payload/timing per region first (rather than guessing where the cost was), which immediately surfaced Kebbi as a 15-300x outlier versus the other two regions, then traced that outlier to its actual cause (a few very long, densely-vertexed representative segments) before reaching for a fix, and verified the fix's expected benefit locally (Python Douglas-Peucker on the real problem segment) before writing any SQL — consistent with this project's established "measure, don't assume" discipline applied here to a performance question rather than a correctness one.

### 2026-09-17 (7) — `UnifiedMap` was never project-aware at all: selecting a Project on `/dashboard` did nothing to the map

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`

**What the user reported:** selecting "Coastal Road" from the Top Projects bar chart — the map still showed cluster bubbles.

**Root-caused deeper than the (6) entry's own fix, not assumed to be the same bug**: `UnifiedMap`'s `Props` interface had no `project` field at all, and `dashboard/page.tsx` never passed one — confirmed by reading both files, not inferred. The map's own reports fetch (`/api/map`) has always requested `all=1` (every project's reports merged together) unconditionally, regardless of which project the rest of the dashboard was filtered to; `hasActiveFilter` in both clusterers (reports, and the (6) entry's road-asset fix) only ever checked `category`/chainage/section — `project` was invisible to the map's logic on every axis, not just clustering. Selecting a project correctly narrowed every other panel (KPIs, charts, report table) via the normal `/api/dashboard?project=...` refetch, but the map component had no way to know that had happened.

**Confirmed scope with the user before implementing** (`AskUserQuestion`): should a project selection also narrow *which* pins the map shows (matching every other filtered view on the page), or only change whether things cluster while still showing every project's pins? User chose the former — full consistency with the rest of the dashboard.

**Fix**: added `project?: string` to `UnifiedMap`'s props, passed from `dashboard/page.tsx` as `data.activeFilters.filterProject`. The reports-fetch effect now drops `all=1` and sends the real `project` value instead of the hardcoded `COASTAL_PROJECT` default whenever a project is selected — `/api/map`'s own existing project-scoping (`.ilike('project_name', ...)`, the same convention `/api/progress` already uses) does the actual narrowing, so this reused existing server-side logic rather than adding new filtering. The category-filter camera-fit logic (previously keyed only on `category`) now fires on either category or project changing, computed off the same just-fetched response as before (not a separate race-prone effect, same discipline as the original 2026-09-15 fix). Both clusterers' `hasActiveFilter` now also include `!!project`.

**Deliberately not attempted**: scoping the road-asset (Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi) fetch itself by `project` — that table's `project` column (`"Coastal road"` / `"Kebbi - Sokoto project"`) is a completely different scoping concept from the reports side's `project_name`, with no reliable mapping between them for arbitrary project names (see the 2026-08-05 changelog's whole "two naming schemes don't overlap" story). Per the (6) entry's already-confirmed behavior, a project selection still counts as "a filter is active" for the asset clusterer (de-clustering it), without attempting to also narrow which asset points are shown.

**Verified live**: navigating to `/dashboard?project=Coastal Road` and inspecting real `/api/map` network calls confirmed `all` is no longer sent and `project=Coastal Road` is sent instead (the separate, unrelated Kebbi-corridor-line fetch — a different `/api/map` call entirely — was unaffected, confirming no regression there). A screenshot confirmed "Top Projects by Reports" correctly shows only Coastal Road (9,724 · 100%) and the map shows the Coastal corridor as a thin highlighted line with no bubbled clusters anywhere, matching what selecting a project does on every other part of the dashboard. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean.

**Why:** Direct user follow-up after the (6) entry's fix didn't resolve their actual scenario — investigated by re-reading the component's props/fetch logic rather than assuming (6)'s fix was simply incomplete in the same way, which is what surfaced that `project` had never been wired through the map at all, a materially bigger gap than a missing filter-check line. The scope question (narrow pins vs. just decluster) was confirmed with the user rather than assumed, since narrowing pins meant reusing `/api/map`'s existing project-scoping in a new way, not a trivial addition.

### 2026-09-17 (6) — `UnifiedMap`: road-asset clusters now also de-cluster when any dashboard filter is active

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`

**What the user asked:** why does the main dashboard's map still show numbered cluster bubbles once a filter is applied.

**Root-caused live before assuming it was the same bug as the 2026-09-15 fix**: reading the component found two structurally separate `MarkerClusterer` instances — one for activity-report pins (`reportClustererRef`), one for road-asset survey points (`assetClustererRef`, Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi). The 2026-09-15 changelog's "zoom to the filtered point(s), no clusters" fix only ever touched the reports clusterer (swapping it to `NoopAlgorithm` when a category/chainage filter is active) — the road-assets clusterer's render effect (`UnifiedMap.tsx:621-654` at the time) never referenced any filter state at all, always using the default clustering algorithm regardless.

**A real data-semantics wrinkle surfaced before fixing, not glossed over**: `road_assets` has no `activity_category` column and its own fetch effect (`UnifiedMap.tsx:287-317`) only depends on which region layers are toggled on and the map viewport — never on `category`/`chFrom`/`chTo`. So a Coastal-Road category filter doesn't actually narrow which asset points are fetched; only a Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi section filter (`initialSection`) does. Asked the user directly (`AskUserQuestion`) whether "filtered" for this layer should mean only section changes, or any active filter at all for whole-map visual consistency — user chose the latter, explicitly trading off a data-semantics distinction a viewer wouldn't necessarily notice anyway in favor of one consistent behavior.

**Fix**: the asset-clustering effect now computes the same `hasActiveFilter` shape the reports effect already does (`!!category || chActive`), extended with `|| !!initialSection`, and swaps its `MarkerClusterer` to `NoopAlgorithm` under the identical destroy-and-recreate-on-mode-flip pattern the reports layer already established (new `assetClusterModeRef`, mirroring `reportClusterModeRef`) — copied for consistency, not reinvented.

**Verified live**: a scripted Playwright pass compared `/dashboard` unfiltered vs. `/dashboard?category=Earthworks` — before, the map showed numbered bubble clusters (19, 3, 2) near Sokoto/Kebbi; after, those same 9 real asset points (confirmed via the unchanged "9 asset points" KPI — the underlying data genuinely didn't change, only the rendering did) no longer bubble. Zero console errors either way. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean.

**Why:** Direct user report investigated by reading the actual clustering code rather than assumed to be a repeat of the already-fixed reports-layer bug — finding the two clusterers were genuinely separate, never-unified code paths is what surfaced the real (and only-then-obvious) data-semantics question, which was confirmed with the user rather than decided unilaterally, consistent with this project's established pattern for exactly this kind of "which of two reasonable behaviors" fork.

### 2026-09-17 (5) — Fix `/road-corridors` feeling slow during interactive zoom: debounce the map's `idle` event, guard against stale out-of-order responses

**Files changed:** `src/components/RoadCorridorMap.tsx`

**What the user reported:** the map "taking so much to render," with a screenshot of a sparse (non-hotspot) Ogun view.

**Investigated rather than assumed the (4) entry's pagination/hotspot fix was the cause**: reproduced a comparably sparse location directly — real query time there measured 321ms for 49 segments, ruling out the query itself as the bottleneck for a typical (non-hotspot) view. Reading `RoadCorridorMap.tsx`'s fetch effect found a real, separate architectural gap instead: `google.maps.Map`'s `idle` event fires after **every discrete pan/zoom step**, not just the final settled view — a user zooming in via several quick scroll/double-click actions was triggering one full fetch-and-rebuild-all-polylines cycle **per step**, with no debounce and no guard against a slower, earlier request resolving after a newer one. Overlapping cycles could apply out of order (a stale response's polylines replacing a newer, already-correct render), and — worse after the (4) entry's pagination fix — each individual step at close zoom could independently trigger its own up-to-5-page DB round trip, multiplying redundant server load during normal interactive use. This is the same class of bug this project's own history has hit and fixed elsewhere (`requestIdRef` in `/dashboard`'s `loadData`, various guards in `UnifiedMap`) — `RoadCorridorMap` never got the same treatment when it was first built.

**Fix**: (1) the `idle` listener now debounces 350ms before updating `viewState`, so a rapid multi-step zoom/pan sequence collapses into one fetch for wherever the user actually stops, instead of one fetch per intermediate step; (2) a `reqIdRef` generation counter on the fetch effect discards any response that resolves after a newer request has already started, so a slow stale fetch can never overwrite a fresher render (applied to the success, error, and loading-state paths, not just the happy path).

**Verified live**: a scripted 5-step rapid zoom+pan sequence (ending at a genuinely new, previously-unfetched viewport, via the same `window.__debugCorridorMap` debug hook added in the (4) entry) fired exactly **1** `/api/road-corridors` request — confirmed via network-request interception, not inferred from timing alone.
