Also measured, for context: a genuine maximum-density hotspot (5,000 paginated segments) takes ~1.2–2s end-to-end (server query + network + building 5,000 `Polyline` objects) — a real, bounded cost only reached at the single most extreme known location, not the common case. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean, zero console errors in the verification pass.

**Why:** Direct user report of sluggishness, investigated by first ruling out the query layer (measured fast at a comparable location) before looking at the render/fetch cycle's own logic, where a real, demonstrable gap was found — consistent with this project's "measure before concluding" discipline rather than assuming the most recently-shipped fix (pagination) was automatically the cause just because it was the most recent change.

### 2026-09-17 (4) — `/road-corridors` still "not so compact" after the (3) fix: keyset pagination past PostgREST's 1000-row cap, plus a real `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` overload bug

**Files changed:** `scripts/sql/add_road_corridors_infra.sql`, `src/app/api/road-corridors/route.ts`, `src/components/RoadCorridorMap.tsx`

**What the user reported:** after the (3) entry's fix, a zoomed-in Calabar screenshot still showed visible gaps between clusters of lines — "still not so compact" — compared to the ArcGIS Pro reference.

**Root-caused live, not assumed already fixed:** the (3) fix's close-zoom detail path was correct but still capped at PostgREST's hard 1000-row response ceiling. Located the user's actual hotspot programmatically (no coordinates were given) by pulling all 1,120 Calabar `road_corridors_grid` rows and bucketing them into coarser cells to find where real fine-grid cells cluster densest, then confirmed live: a 555m-radius box there returns exactly 1000 (the cap) while narrower boxes centered nearby return only 5-10 — meaning the true hotspot is tiny (its returned fragments' own lng/lat range was only ~90m × 155m) and almost certainly holds many more than 1000 real fragments, with the unordered `LIMIT` returning an arbitrary, patchy subset of them.

**Fixed with keyset pagination**: added a `p_after_id bigint` cursor parameter to `road_corridor_segments()`'s close-zoom path (`WHERE id > p_after_id ORDER BY id LIMIT LEAST(p_max_segments, 1000)`), and the API route now loops up to 5 pages (5,000 fragments max) advancing the cursor each time, stopping early once a page returns fewer than 1000 rows (bbox exhausted). Manually paginating (not relying on PostgREST's `.range()`/`Range` header against the RPC result) was a deliberate choice — this project's own history (2026-07-22 "map freezing" entry) already found `.range()` unreliable for raising the effective row count on an RPC call, so keyset pagination via an explicit WHERE-clause cursor was used instead, which is correct by construction regardless of how PostgREST slices RPC results.

**Before committing to this design, actually measured whether `ORDER BY id` was cheap enough** — the (3) entry's SQL comment had claimed "tested both ways, ORDER BY forces a full sort" without having actually run that version live, which was inaccurate self-documentation, corrected here: a real `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` (user-run, not assumed) on the hotspot's own bbox showed `Sort Method: top-N heapsort`, 175.8ms total, over a candidate set of only ~1,515 bbox-filtered rows — the earlier "would force a full sort of thousands+ rows" concern didn't hold at the geographic scale a real close-zoom bbox actually covers, since dense hotspots tend to be small in area even when very dense in content.

**A second real bug hit while applying this SQL change, not present in the final state**: `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` only replaces a function whose *parameter signature* (types + count) matches exactly — adding `p_after_id` changed the signature, so Postgres silently created a **second overloaded function** instead of replacing the original 7-parameter one. PostgREST then couldn't resolve which overload to call for any request omitting the new parameter, surfacing live as `PGRST203 "Could not choose the best candidate function"` on every real map request (caught via a live Playwright pass showing genuine console 503s, investigated via the server log rather than assumed to be the same class of bug as before). Fixed with an explicit `DROP FUNCTION ... (text, double precision, double precision, double precision, double precision, double precision, int)` (matching the old signature) before the `CREATE OR REPLACE` — noted in the SQL file as a rule for any future signature change to this function, not just this one instance.

**Added `window.__debugCorridorMap`** (`RoadCorridorMap.tsx`) exposing the live `google.maps.Map` instance — same established convention as `UnifiedMap`'s `window.__debugHighlightLines`/`__debugDesignLines` — specifically because blind UI-driven zoom (double-click/scroll-wheel simulation) proved unreliable for navigating a Playwright browser to an exact known coordinate during this verification; the debug hook let a script call `setCenter`/`setZoom` directly against the real hotspot location found above.

**Verified live, end-to-end**: direct RPC pagination test confirmed 6 sequential pages each returned exactly 1000 unique, non-overlapping fragment ids (6,000 total, zero duplicates — the keyset cursor is correct) before hitting the test's own page limit, confirming the true hotspot density exceeds even the route's 5,000-row cap. The full `/api/road-corridors` route, called directly at the hotspot's bbox, returned exactly 5,000 segments in ~1.8-2s. A scripted Playwright pass using the new debug hook navigated directly to the hotspot's real coordinates (found programmatically, not guessed) and captured a screenshot showing a solid, tightly-packed bundle of parallel lines with no visible gaps at 50m map scale — matching the character of the source ArcGIS Pro data far more closely than either the original "broken" state or the (3) entry's still-patchy fix. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both clean.

**Why:** Direct user follow-up with a fresh screenshot after the (3) fix had already shipped — treated as a real, unresolved report rather than assumed already covered by the prior fix, and investigated by first finding the actual hotspot location programmatically (the user's screenshot carried no coordinates) rather than guessing at an arbitrary test location. Two further real bugs (the SQL comment's unverified "tested both ways" claim, and the `CREATE OR REPLACE` overload gotcha) were caught specifically because this pass insisted on measuring live behavior after each change rather than trusting the previous entry's stated reasoning or assuming a schema change would "just work."

### 2026-09-17 (3) — Fix `/road-corridors` rendering "broken" (sparse, disconnected dashes) at close zoom, and a second summary-query timeout the in-process cache alone couldn't fully cover

**Files changed:** `scripts/sql/add_road_corridors_infra.sql`

**What the user reported:** a screenshot of the live `/road-corridors` map at close zoom showing isolated, disconnected diagonal magenta dashes with large gaps between them, next to a screenshot of the same underlying shapefile data open in ArcGIS Pro showing a dense, tightly-packed bundle of many parallel lines — asking why the rendered map looked broken relative to the source data.

**Root-caused live before touching any code, not assumed:** `road_corridors_grid` (the cache table added in the 2026-09-17 (2) entry below to make `road_corridor_segments()` fast) has a fixed 0.001°-degree (~111m) base cell size — a hard ceiling on detail that no amount of zooming can get past, since that decimation happened once, permanently, when the cache was populated. Confirmed by direct measurement: a real ~1km × 1km Kebbi bbox holds **14,457 raw fragments** in `road_corridors`, but the grid-cache path could only ever surface **18** of them there, at any zoom. Worse, the grid cache's "keep the single longest fragment per cell" heuristic is actively wrong for this data's real shape (median fragment = 2 points, most genuinely short) — it biases toward the rare long outliers and discards the dense short fragments that give the real geometry its continuous, bundled look, which is exactly what made the map look "broken" next to the source ArcGIS Pro data.

**Fix — a genuine close-zoom detail path in `road_corridor_segments()`**: once a caller requests detail finer than or equal to the grid cache's base resolution (`p_grid_deg <= 0.001` — exactly the finest tier `gridDegForZoom()` ever requests, zoom > 14) with a reasonably-sized bbox (a defensive ≤0.1°/~11km-per-side cap), the function now skips `road_corridors_grid` entirely and scans the raw `road_corridors` table directly within that bbox — no per-cell winner-take-all, every real fragment in view has an equal chance of being kept. Written as a genuinely separate query body (not one query branching on a bare boolean `OR`), specifically to avoid the "`OR` in the `WHERE` clause defeats the GIST index" bug this project has now hit twice on this exact table (see the 2026-07-30 `streetlights_cluster` changelog for the first instance) — the bbox is unconditional in this path, so `idx_road_corridors_geom` can always be used.

**A second real, independent bug found while verifying the above**, not present in the eventual fix: re-testing the fully-fixed `/road-corridors` page via a live Playwright pass showed the same class of 503 the 2026-09-17 (2) entry's in-process `cachedSummary()` fix was supposed to have already closed. Investigated rather than assumed fixed: the server log showed a genuine Postgres `57014` (`statement_timeout`), and direct re-measurement of `road_corridor_summary()` in isolation (no concurrency at all) showed **4.87–7.68s per call** — uncomfortably close to, and on one observed run *over*, the `authenticator` role's 8s `statement_timeout`, for a single request. The in-process request-dedup from the (2) entry reduced how *often* this query ran, but couldn't fix that the underlying query itself was already marginal — deduping three risky calls into one still-risky call doesn't remove the risk. Fixed by adding `road_corridors_summary_cache` — a 3-row table populated once via a manual `INSERT` in the SQL editor (no 8s cap there), with `road_corridor_summary()` now just reading it. The original 2026-09-17 (2) comment claiming "a plain GROUP BY is fine here, no cache table needed" was a wrong guess, corrected here by actually measuring rather than assuming the earlier reasoning still held once real concurrency and real Playwright traffic hit it.

**Also tightened, a pure efficiency fix with no behavior change**: discovered live that PostgREST hard-caps every response at 1,000 rows project-wide (the same well-documented ceiling this project has hit repeatedly elsewhere), so the close-zoom detail path's internal `LIMIT` is now `LEAST(p_max_segments, 1000)` rather than computing/transferring rows Postgres would only have had silently discarded downstream. Considered and rejected adding `ORDER BY id` for deterministic row selection across calls — tested directly and confirmed it forces a full sort of every bbox match before `LIMIT` can apply (the GIST index doesn't return rows in id order), turning a bounded LIMIT-scoped scan into one bounded by the true match count instead (14,457 in the measured case) — a real, tested-for regression, not a hypothetical. Left unordered; which exact 1,000 of e.g. 14,457 fragments come back can vary slightly call to call, a minor accepted cosmetic tradeoff since the actual reported bug (a sparse, broken-looking corridor) no longer depends on it either way.

**Verified live, end-to-end, after every fix**: direct RPC re-tests confirmed the tight bbox now returns 1,000 real fragments (up from 18) in ~2s, a wider ~5km bbox also returns 1,000 in ~0.5s, the whole-region overview path is unaffected, and an oversized (>0.1°) bbox safely falls back to the grid-cache path rather than attempting an unbounded raw scan. `road_corridor_summary()` now returns in 0.22–0.98s (down from 4.87–7.68s) with identical numbers. A scripted Playwright pass against a genuinely fresh `next start` (confirmed by PID, not assumed) drove the live page through a region switch and a real zoom+pan sequence: 0 console errors, the "Rendered on Map" KPI showing 1,000 at the deepest zoom tested, and a screenshot showing a real dense, tightly-packed zigzag bundle of segments — matching the character of the source ArcGIS Pro data, not the earlier sparse/disconnected look. `tsc --noEmit` clean (no TypeScript changes this pass — every fix in this entry is SQL-only).

**Why:** Direct user report with a side-by-side screenshot comparison, which is exactly the kind of concrete evidence this project's "measure, don't assume" discipline is built around — the root cause (a fixed-resolution cache table's detail ceiling, combined with a decimation heuristic that's wrong for this specific data's fragment-size distribution) was confirmed with a real SQL count (14,457 vs 18) before any fix was written, and the second bug (the summary query's real marginal cost) was only found because verification was actually re-run against a genuinely fresh server rather than assumed still-fixed from the earlier entry.

### 2026-09-17 (2) — `/road-corridors` rebuilt: direct shapefile ingestion + a live Google Maps view, replacing the Earth Engine iframe below

**Files changed:** `src/app/road-corridors/page.tsx` (rewritten), `src/components/RoadCorridorMap.tsx` (new), `src/app/api/road-corridors/route.ts` (new), `sync_road_corridors.py` (new), `scripts/sql/add_road_corridors_infra.sql` (new)

**Why this supersedes the entry immediately below:** after the iframe version shipped, the user was explicit that it wasn't what they wanted — *"I don't just want you to embed it in the dashboard as a google earth map, I need you to have access to the shapefiles directly, create a live dashboard, proper filters and all, with google earth engine as the map."* Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` that "Google Earth Engine as the map" meant reusing this project's existing Google Maps satellite/hybrid basemap (no real Earth Engine API credentials exist for this project), and that ingestion should be a full one-time load (same convention as `road_assets`/`streetlights`), not a sampled subset.

**Real data-quality finding, checked live before writing any ingestion code, not assumed from record counts alone:** all three source shapefiles (`Downloads/shapefiles/Calabar_shapefile/Calabar_All.shp`, `Ogun_shapefile/Ogun_All.shp`, `Kebbi/Kebbi/Kebbi_ready.shp`) turned out to be the same underlying problem packaged two different ways — `Kebbi_ready.shp` is 3,303,045 separate *records*, while `Calabar_All.shp`/`Ogun_All.shp` each report as a single record but that one record has 1,080,386 / 490,034 *parts* respectively. An early pass assumed the single-record files were "already clean" purely from `len(sf) == 1` — checking `shp.parts` directly (before writing the ingestion script, not after) caught that all three are the same CAD-export artifact: one continuous road alignment exploded into a separate 2-point-median-length fragment per vertex-to-vertex span, no ordering/connectivity preserved. All three confirmed `EPSG:3857` (Web Mercator) via `.prj`, verified by reprojecting each file's bbox center and checking it landed on the already-known `REGION_CAMERA` coordinates in `UnifiedMap.tsx` — all matched within the expected margin, so a plain closed-form Web Mercator → WGS84 formula was used (no GDAL/proj4 dependency).

**A real closure/scoping bug found and fixed via a dry run, before the real ~75-minute ingestion, not after:** the first version of `region_segments()` (`sync_road_corridors.py`) used a generator expression `(all_points[bounds[i]:bounds[i+1]] for i in range(len(bounds)-1))` whose loop variable in the consuming `for` loop was also named `pts` — shadowing the same name used elsewhere in the enclosing scope. Since a generator expression's body is evaluated lazily on each pull (closing over the enclosing scope by reference, not by value at definition time), the first outer-loop iteration's reassignment of `pts` silently corrupted every subsequent slice, collapsing ~all of Calabar/Ogun's 1M+ real parts down to near-nothing. Caught by re-running a dry-run diagnostic against locally-extracted copies of the shapefiles (no Supabase writes) before the real run — fixed by renaming the outer full-point-list variable to `all_points` and the loop variable to `seg_pts`, verified via a second dry run showing correct, distinct real geometry for all three regions.

**Ingestion**: one cleaning rule, applied uniformly (not per-region): a 2-point segment longer than 5,000m is dropped as a "jump artifact," not real geometry — confirmed live these exist (Kebbi alone has ~14 dead-straight lines spanning 20–116km, clearly spurious) and confirmed the threshold is generous enough to never remove legitimate geometry (Calabar/Ogun's longest real 2-point segments never exceed ~1.8km). Considered and rejected deduplicating groups of near-identical-length long segments as likely "duplicate CAD exports" — reconsidered given this project's own established convention (`road_assets` tracks `LHS`/`RHS`/`Median` as genuinely separate real lines for the same road) that near-identical parallel lines are more likely real distinct carriageway/median geometry than duplicates, and there's no attribute field here to disambiguate either way; shipped without speculative deduplication rather than risk silently discarding real geometry. Real ingestion run: 4,873,459 segments inserted (Calabar 1,080,386 / Ogun 490,034 / Kebbi 3,303,039) in ~77 minutes total, batched 1000-at-a-time with a retry-with-backoff on transient insert failures.

**`scripts/sql/add_road_corridors_infra.sql`**: `road_corridors` table (`geometry(LineString, 4326)`, `region`/`length_m`/`npoints`) + `road_corridor_summary()` (real `COUNT`/`SUM` aggregate, `region`-indexed) + `road_corridor_segments()` (viewport/zoom-scoped decimated sample, one representative-by-length segment per grid cell — same "coarse then refined" pattern as `map_chainage_line`/`streetlights_cluster`/`road_assets_cluster`). **Two real bugs found and fixed live, after real data existed, both documented in full as comments in the SQL file:**
1. A PL/pgSQL variable-shadowing bug: `RETURNS TABLE(..., length_m numeric, ...)` implicitly declares `length_m` as a function-body variable, which made a bare `order by length_m desc` inside the function genuinely ambiguous (Postgres error 42702) — the function errored on every call once real data existed, never returning rows at all. Fixed by fully qualifying every column reference (`candidates.length_m`, `ranked.length_m`, etc.).
2. After that fix, `road_corridor_segments()` still timed out live (57014, the 8s `statement_timeout`) on **every** call tested, including a real bbox-scoped Kebbi viewport query — the exact same "OR in the WHERE clause defeats the GIST index" bug this project already hit and fixed once on `streetlights_cluster` (2026-07-30 changelog), plus a more fundamental problem: the whole-region no-bbox path (which is not a rare case — it's the very first fetch every region always makes, since `RoadCorridorMap`'s viewport state starts `null` before any Google Maps `idle` event fires) has no bbox to narrow a multi-million-row scan from at all. Fixed with a new small cache table, `road_corridors_grid` — one representative-by-length segment per 0.001°-grid cell (~111m), populated **once** via a manual `INSERT ... SELECT` run directly in the SQL editor (no 8s cap there, unlike the RPC path — same precedent as this project's other large one-time table operations). Collapsed 4,873,459 raw rows to 5,061 grid cells (Calabar 1,120 / Ogun 384 / Kebbi 3,557) — `road_corridor_segments()` now always reads this small table, never the raw one, so neither failure mode can recur regardless of whether the planner uses an index against it.

**A third real bug, found live via a Playwright pass, not assumed away**: `GET /api/road-corridors` called `road_corridor_summary()` (the real aggregate over the raw 4.87M-row table, ~5s per call) on *every* request. `/road-corridors` defaults to "All Regions," which fires 3 concurrent per-region fetches on first load — 3 concurrent ~5s full-table scans against this Supabase instance's constrained resources (small `work_mem`/`shared_buffers`, few parallel workers, the same limits this project's history has repeatedly measured) blew past the 8s timeout and surfaced as real live 503s, confirmed via a scripted Playwright pass showing genuine console errors, not a hypothetical. **One false alarm during this fix, worth recording**: after adding an in-process cache and rebuilding, a second Playwright run still showed the same 503s — turned out to be a zombie `next start` from a moment earlier still bound to the test port serving the pre-fix build (the exact same "stale server looks like a live regression" gotcha the 2026-09-08 (2) shell-pass entry already documents), not a real failure of the fix. Confirmed by explicitly killing the PID actually listening on the port and starting a genuinely fresh server, then re-testing — 0 console errors, and a direct concurrent-curl test against the confirmed-fresh server showed all 3 requests taking ~5s each (not 3×5s sequential), confirming they really did share one deduped query via `cachedSummary()` rather than firing 3 independent ones.

**`src/components/RoadCorridorMap.tsx`**: a dedicated map component (deliberately not `UnifiedMap`, which is tightly coupled to the dashboard/planning-implementation's reports+road_assets concerns) — standard Google Maps loader pattern, region colors matched to the original Earth Engine app's own scheme for visual continuity (`ogun: '#d4ff00'` neon yellow / `calabar: '#22e5ff'` cyan / `kebbi: '#ff2ec8'` magenta, confirmed live from the app being replaced), viewport-driven fetch effect, region-change camera fit with a seed-and-skip guard so a fresh page load doesn't yank the initial view, and click-to-popup on each rendered polyline (length, vertex count).

**`src/app/road-corridors/page.tsx`**: region filter pills (All/Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi), real KPI cards (segment count, total length, regions covered, map load time) sourced from `road_corridor_summary()` — not fabricated, unlike some of this project's other coverage pages' planned/synthetic figures. Kept the "Compare with the original Earth Engine app ↗" link from the superseded version as a secondary reference, plus a disclosure line clarifying the map only ever renders a bounded, zoom-scoped sample of the real ingested data (never all ~4.87M segments at once).

**Verified live, end-to-end, not just built**: `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (26 routes). Direct SQL/RPC verification of every stage (raw table row counts, grid cache population, both whole-region and bbox-scoped `road_corridor_segments()` calls under real concurrency). A scripted Playwright pass against a confirmed-fresh `next start`: page loads with 0 console errors, region pills switch correctly (Kebbi KPI card updates to real Kebbi numbers), the map renders real satellite imagery with a correctly-colored polyline following the actual Kebbi road alignment through the terrain (confirmed by screenshot, not just a row count), and the "Rendered on Map"/"Query Time" KPIs reflect real per-request numbers (93 segments / 369ms in one observed load).

**Why:** Direct follow-up ask after the simpler iframe version (below) was explicitly rejected as insufficient — the user wanted real data ownership (their own shapefiles, not someone else's hosted app) and a genuinely filterable, live dashboard. Every fix in this entry was driven by something actually measured or actually failing (a dry-run test catching the closure bug before a 75-minute production run could be corrupted by it; two SQL bugs caught by real RPC calls erroring/timing out against real data, not assumed correct from reading the SQL; the summary-caching bug caught by a live Playwright pass showing real console errors), consistent with this project's own repeatedly-established "verify against real data and real live behavior, don't assume" discipline.

### 2026-09-17 — New `/road-corridors` page: embeds a public Google Earth Engine App

**Files changed:** `src/app/road-corridors/page.tsx` (new), `src/components/SideNav.tsx`

**What the user asked for:** visualize `https://academic-matter-367309.projects.earthengine.app/view/roadcorridors` (a road-corridor satellite viewer built on Google Earth Engine) on a new dashboard page.

**Confirmed embeddable before building anything, not assumed:** `curl -D -` against the app URL showed no `X-Frame-Options` and no `Content-Security-Policy` header at all — Earth Engine Apps are built by Google specifically to be shareable/embeddable, and this one carries nothing that would block being iframed. Given that, the page is a direct `<iframe src="https://academic-matter-367309.projects.earthengine.app/view/roadcorridors">` — no proxy, no scraping, no API route, since there's no Hitech data involved and nothing to fetch from Supabase.

**`/road-corridors` page**: self-contained, matches the achromatic shell's page-header convention (`<h2>`/`<p>`, `28px 36px` padding) rather than the older skeuomorphic `Panel` pattern other pages still carry — this page has no filters/KPIs/tables to justify that heavier chrome, just the embed. A loading spinner overlay shows until the iframe's own `onLoad` fires (Earth Engine Apps take a couple of seconds to initialize their own map/UI inside the frame), an "Open in Earth Engine" link opens the app directly in a new tab as a fallback, and a small mono disclaimer line makes clear this is third-party content that doesn't go through this dashboard's own API — consistent with how `/api/road-assets-coverage`'s fabricated-data disclosures are handled elsewhere in this codebase (flag it plainly rather than let it pass as native).

**Real, useful discovery while verifying, not just a smoke test:** the embedded app itself is titled "Road Corridor Monitoring — High-visibility corridor alignment viewer" with a layer toggle for exactly **Ogun (Neon Yellow) / Calabar (Cyan) / Kebbi (Magenta)** — the same three regions `/road-assets-coverage` and `UnifiedMap`'s asset layers already track. This is a companion satellite view of the same road-corridor project, not an unrelated map — worth knowing if this ever needs deeper integration (e.g. linking a section filter into the EE app's own "Zoom to Project Corridor" control) rather than a plain embed.

**Added to `SideNav`** under the existing "Coverage" group, alongside "Asset Coverage" (new `IconSatellite`, same lucide-approximate line-icon convention as every other sidebar icon). `DashHeader` needed no change — it dropped its own nav-link list in the 2026-09-07 shell rebuild; the rail covers both desktop and the mobile drawer now.

**One console message seen during verification, not a bug in this integration:** the embedded app itself throws a CORS error trying to reach `accounts.google.com`/`uc.appengine.google.com` for what looks like a sign-in/project-metadata check — this comes from *inside* the Earth Engine app's own JS, is the expected behaviour for an anonymous viewer with no active Google session in that browser context (which is exactly how a public EE App is meant to be used), and does not stop the map from rendering — confirmed via screenshot showing the fully-loaded map, layer controls, and base-map selector.

**Verified live:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (25 routes, `/road-corridors` static). Playwright pass against `next start` with a minted session: nav link present and correctly highlighted active on `/road-corridors`, the loading spinner clears once the iframe's `onLoad` fires, and a full-page screenshot confirms the real Earth Engine UI (corridor-monitoring panel, layer checkboxes, zoom/search controls) renders inside the frame, not a blank/blocked embed.

### 2026-09-15 (2) — `GET /api/map`: fix the 1000-row PostgREST cap silently truncating report queries

**Files changed:** `src/app/api/map/route.ts`

**What changed:** `fetchReports()`'s three queries all used `.limit(5000)`/`.limit(1000)`, but PostgREST hard-caps every single response at 1000 rows project-wide regardless of the requested limit (documented since the 2026-07-22 "map freezing" entry, but never fixed here). Confirmed live before touching anything: Coastal `activity_category = 'Earthworks'` has 1,788 real matching reports; the old query silently returned only the first 1,000 (56%) — this fed directly into the zoom/no-cluster work from the entry above, so a broad category filter was fitting the camera and rendering pins from a truncated 56% of the real matches, silently. Fixed with the same page-until-exhausted `fetchAll()` pattern `src/app/api/progress/route.ts` already uses (`.range()` in a loop, defensive 30-page/30,000-row ceiling — `hitech_report_hitechreport` is ~9.7k rows, comfortably fine to page through in full, two-plus orders of magnitude smaller than the tables this project's other routes explicitly avoid `fetchAll()`-ing). Verified live: the same Earthworks query now returns 1,793 rows (1,788 Coastal + a handful from other regions matching the same category).

**Real trade-off, measured, not glossed over:** the fully-unfiltered `all=1` fetch (used on a fresh `/dashboard` load with no category set) now takes ~3.8s to page through all 9,746 rows, versus a sub-second response when it was silently capped at 1,000. This is the honest cost of correctness here — accepted rather than special-cased away, consistent with this project's existing precedent of accepting multi-second loads on this dataset when correctness is otherwise at stake (`/progress` ~5–8s, Kebbi `/road-assets-coverage` ~10–12s). Narrower category filters (the case that was actually silently wrong) stay fast — only categories with hundreds+ of matches pay for more than one page.

**Known follow-up, not fixed this pass:** the reports side of `/api/map` has never been bbox-scoped (only the `map_chainage_line` stations RPC is) — deliberately, since Coastal reports' raw `start_chainage_lat/long` is known-unreliable (thousands share a handful of stuck/bogus GPS values, see the 2026-07-22 chainage-snapping entry), so bbox-filtering on raw GPS would incorrectly hide reports that only land in the right place after the frontend's nearest-station snap. Because `UnifiedMap`'s fetch-effect cache key includes the viewport bbox (to trigger station-line refinement on pan/zoom), a broad/unfiltered report set now gets **re-fetched in full on every meaningful pan once zoomed past z12**, at the new ~3.8s cost each time, not just once. In practice this only bites a user who both zooms in past 12 *and* has no/a broad category filter active — the persisted default Coastal camera sits at z11, so it doesn't trigger for typical browsing — but it's a real, not-yet-addressed cost, flagged here rather than silently left for someone else to rediscover. A proper fix would decouple the stations (bbox-keyed) and reports (category/project-keyed only) fetches instead of one shared endpoint/cache-key driving both.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean. Direct `curl` against a local dev server with a minted session: unfiltered `all=1` → 9,746 reports in ~3.8s (was silently 1,000); `category=Earthworks` → 1,793 reports (was silently 1,000).

### 2026-09-15 — `UnifiedMap`: a category/chainage filter now zooms to the real matching location and shows individual pins, not a cluster bubble

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`

**What the user asked for:** when a dashboard filter is applied, the map should zoom in on the matching point(s) with no clustering — i.e. show each matching report as its own pin rather than a bubbled count.

**Real bug found first, not assumed:** verified live (Playwright, real UI click on the Category `<select>`, not a URL-param page load) that selecting a category barely moved the camera at all — it panned a few degrees toward roughly the national-default centroid instead of the category's real location. Root cause: the existing "fit to filter" effect computed bounds from the `reports` **state**, keyed only on `category` changing — but `category` changing also triggers a separate fetch effect that re-populates `reports` *asynchronously*. The fit effect fires immediately on the prop change, before that fetch resolves, so it builds bounds from whatever `reports` happened to hold from the *previous* filter (the full un-filtered ~9.7k set on first change) — a real race, not a hypothetical. Fixed by computing the category fit **inside the fetch's own `.then()`**, off the just-received response (`d.reports`) directly, instead of a separate effect reading React state that might not have caught up yet. Verified after the fix: selecting "Clear Fence View" (111 real Coastal reports, confirmed via direct Supabase query) now lands the camera at zoom 11 centered on 6.43/3.67 — right on the real data — versus zoom 6 / national-centroid before.

**Chainage filter previously didn't filter the map's reports at all.** `chFrom`/`chTo` only ever drove the road-line highlight color and the camera-fit bounds (via `coastalStations`) — the actual report **pins** shown were never narrowed by chainage, only by category. Fixed: the report-pins render effect now also skips any Coastal-region report whose chainage range doesn't overlap `[chFrom, chTo]` when both are set, so what's plotted actually matches what's highlighted/fitted. The "N reports" readout in the map's own control bar was also silently wrong for this case (always showing the full fetched count, e.g. still "1035" under a chainage filter that only matched 315) — added a `visibleReportCount` state, set from the actual marker/line count each render pass, and pointed the readout at that instead of `reports.length`.

**No clusters when filtered:** the report `MarkerClusterer` is now recreated with `NoopAlgorithm` (from `@googlemaps/markerclusterer`, disables all bubbling) whenever a category or a fully-set chainage range is active, and with the normal default (SuperCluster) algorithm otherwise — swapping requires a fresh `MarkerClusterer` instance (the algorithm is constructor-only), tracked via a new `reportClusterModeRef` so it's only torn down and rebuilt on an actual mode flip, not every render.

**Zoom determinism:** both the category and chainage fit paths now count matched points and, for a single point (or a bounds Google reports as zero-area), call `panTo` + `setZoom(16)` directly instead of `fitBounds` — `fitBounds` on a degenerate single-point box doesn't reliably zoom in across Maps JS versions, so this makes "one matching report" behave the same every time rather than depending on that behavior.

**Also fixed while touching the fit logic:** the category bounds calc previously only considered reports where `regionOf(r) === 'reports'` (Coastal) — a category matching *only* reports in Calabar/Kebbi/Ogun would silently fail to fit at all (`has` stayed false). Removed that restriction; the category branch now fits to every matching report regardless of region (the existing Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi section-filter takeover guard is unaffected — a regional section filter still owns the camera ahead of this).

**Verified live:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (24 routes). Scripted Playwright pass against `next start` with a minted session, real UI interaction (not URL params, which hit the intentional "don't yank the view on initial load" seed-and-skip guard by design): selecting "Clear Fence View" via the Category `<select>` → zoom 6→11, centered on the real Coastal location, report count 111 (matches a direct Supabase query for that category), zero console errors; typing a 20000–30000 chainage range → zoom 6→13, report count correctly drops from the full 1035 to 315 with the map visibly showing individual small colored dots strung along the highlighted road segment (confirmed by screenshot, not just the count) rather than one amber bubble.


### 2026-09-09 (3) — `/dashboard`: click a report row → Site Media shows only that report's own submitted photos

**Files changed:** `src/app/api/report-media/route.ts` (new), `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`

**What the user asked for:** clicking a row in "Recent Activity Reports" already zooms the map to that report — they also wanted the Site Media panel to then show *only* the photos/videos submitted on that one report.

**`GET /api/report-media?id=<reportId>`** (new, session-guarded, `Cache-Control: private, max-age=60`): returns `{ items: [{ file, media_type, project_name: '' }] }` straight from `hitech_report_hitechphoto` where `report_id = id` (tiny per report — no RPC/pagination). A dedicated fetch rather than reusing the page's `mediaItems`, because that array carries no `report_id` and, under a broad filter, is a 600-row *recent* sample that often won't contain a given report's photos at all.

**`/dashboard` page:** new `reportMedia` state (`{ id, label, items, loading }`) + `ReportMedia` component. `handleSelectReport` now also sets `reportMedia`, fetches `/api/report-media`, and scrolls the (new `mediaPanelRef`-wrapped) Site Media card into view — the existing map `setFocusRequest` is unchanged. While `reportMedia` is set, the card renders the isolated view (photos/videos `MediaBox`es, or "No photos or videos were submitted on this report", or "Loading…") with a `← All filtered media` action button; it also carries a stale-fetch guard (`prev.id === r.id`). The view clears on that button **or** any filter/nav change (`setReportMedia(null)` at the top of `loadData`).

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (24 routes, `/api/report-media` registered). Query logic checked against live data (report 87701 → 2 photos, 87713 → 1, a report with no rows → `items: []` → the empty state). Not exercised in a browser this pass.

### 2026-09-09 (2) — `/dashboard`: fix Site Media / Recent Reports / Calendar going empty on a filter change, and make the map follow the Section filter

**Files changed:** `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`, `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`

**What the user reported:** with the filter set to `Category = Earthworks · Project = Coastal Road · Section = Section 3 - Calabar`, the top cards correctly showed 10 matched reports / 3 photos / weather 5·3·2, but the **Site Media** panel said "No media matches the active filters", **Recent Activity Reports** said "No reports match your filters", and the **Activity Map** was sitting on Lagos, not Calabar. (The `20000` / `30000` in the chainage inputs are just the `placeholder` hint text — no chainage filter was actually applied. Verified live: that filter combo really does match 10 reports; adding `ch 20000–30000` would drop it to 1.)

**Bug 1 — the empty panels (the real defect).** Verified against the live DB that both RPCs are healthy and agree: `dashboard_core` → `totalReports 10 / totalPhotos 3`, `dashboard_extra` → `recentReports 10 / mediaItems 3 / activityCalendar 5`. The frontend was **throwing the extra payload away**. `loadData` fires `/api/dashboard` (core) and `/api/dashboard/extra` in parallel and merges them; `dashboard_core` is the heavier query, so on a filter change `extra` usually resolves first. The old extra handler only stashed its payload into `pendingExtraRef` when `prev` was `null` (first load) — otherwise it merged straight into state. Then the slower core response rebuilt state as `{ ...EMPTY_HEAVY, ...core }` and, finding nothing in `pendingExtraRef`, **wiped the already-arrived `recentReports` / `mediaItems` / `activityCalendar` back to `[]`**. Fresh full-page loads were fine (the `prev === null` path stashed correctly); only in-app filter changes hit it. Fix: the extra handler now **always** does `pendingExtraRef.current = { reqId, x }`, so the core handler's existing `if (pendingExtraRef.current?.reqId === reqId) Object.assign(merged, …)` folds it back in regardless of arrival order. Traced all 7 orderings (extra-first / core-first / first-load / stale-request / either-fetch-fails) — all land consistent.

**Bug 2 — the map ignoring the Section filter.** `<UnifiedMap>` on `/dashboard` was passed `chFrom`/`chTo`/`category` but never `section`, and its "fit to an active filter" effect only knows Coastal Road chainage stations + Coastal (`regionOf === 'reports'`) report pins — so `category = Earthworks` fitted the camera to Coastal Earthworks reports (Lagos), and the Calabar section filter was invisible to it. Fix: (a) pass `initialSection={data.activeFilters.filterSection}` — the same prop `/planning-implementation` already uses; its existing section-fit effect then recentres to `REGION_CAMERA.calabar` (etc.) when the Section dropdown changes. (b) the category/chainage-fit effect now bails when a regional section (`calabar`/`ogun`/`kebbi`) is active, so it can't drag the view back to Lagos; `initialSection` added to its deps. First-load seed-and-skip behaviour of the section-fit effect is unchanged (a hard refresh with all filters in the URL still opens on the persisted camera — minor, not addressed this pass).

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (23 routes). Backend confirmed healthy/consistent via direct RPC calls against project `cwqfyhapaycabynqwczx` (`dashboard_core` vs `dashboard_extra` vs `dashboard_filtered_ids` all return the same 10-report set for the reported filter). Not re-run in a live browser this pass — the fix is an arrival-order guard in `loadData` and a camera-fit guard, both traced by hand.

### 2026-09-09 — One unified, persistent map: merged HitechMap + RoadAssetsMap, all sections on one map, report-click zooms to the right section

**Files changed:** `src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx` (new — absorbs the two old map components), `src/lib/map-view.tsx` (new), `src/lib/theme-constants.ts` (+`MAP_VIEW_STORAGE_KEY`), `src/app/layout.tsx` (mount `MapViewProvider`), `src/app/api/map/route.ts` (`all=1` scope), `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`, `src/app/planning-implementation/page.tsx`, **deleted** `src/components/HitechMap.tsx` + `src/components/RoadAssetsMap.tsx`.

**What the user asked for:** the dashboard map only knew Coastal Road Section 1B/1C; `/planning-implementation` had a *separate* map for the Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi road_assets. Wanted **one map, used everywhere a map appears, that persists its view across pages**, showing **every section that has geolocation** at once, and — on `/dashboard` — clicking any activity report should zoom to *that report's real section* (a Calabar report → Calabar), not just Section 1. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: shared component + saved camera (not one hoisted instance); all layers on with toggle chips; sections = Coastal 1A/1B/1C/2 + Calabar + Kebbi + Ogun (Ogun ~28% geolocated — show those; "Kogi" was a typo for Kebbi); report-zoom uses the report's own GPS + auto-enables that area's asset layer.

**Data checked live first** (project `cwqfyhapaycabynqwczx`): activity reports *do* carry real GPS for the other regions — 12 `Section 3 - Calabar`, 21 `Kebbi section` (filed under project `SBS ... highway`/`Sokoto road`, not `Coastal Road`), 2 Ogun — all with `start_chainage_lat/long`. `road_assets` lat/lon: Calabar 1.34M / Kebbi 5.53M fully geolocated, **Ogun 289k of 401k NULL**. The `chainages` table (Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi station refs) has **northing/easting only, no lat/lon** — so those 3 get cluster points but no drawn road-alignment line (only Coastal + the Kebbi corridor via `hitech_report_chainage` pid 7 have a line).

**`src/lib/map-view.tsx` — `MapViewProvider` / `useMapView()`** (pattern: `src/lib/sidebar.tsx`), mounted in `layout.tsx` inside `ThemeProvider`→`SidebarProvider`. Holds `layers` (`coastalLine`/`reports`/`calabar`/`ogun`/`kebbi`/`design`, all default-on), `colorBy`, `camera`, and a `focusRequest` (`{lat,lng,zoom,reportId,enableLayer,popup}`) consumed once then cleared. **All of it (camera included) is persisted to `localStorage['hitech-map-view']`** — the app navigates with plain `<a href>` (SideNav/DashHeader are not `<Link>`), so a full page load happens on every nav and React context alone would reset the view each time. `readPersistedCamera()` is a synchronous export because a child effect (UnifiedMap init) runs *before* the provider's hydrate effect.

**`src/components/UnifiedMap.tsx`** — one Google Maps `hybrid` instance, `dynamic(ssr:false)` at both call sites. Layers, all `idle`/viewport-driven:
- **Coastal line + 1km ticks + report pins** — one `/api/map?all=1&project=Coastal Road&<bbox>` call. Section 1 reports snap to the nearest sampled chainage station (unchanged from the old HitechMap — stale Coastal GPS, see 2026-07-22); Calabar/Kebbi/Ogun reports (detected by section/project keyword in `regionOf()`) render at their raw GPS.
- **Kebbi corridor line** — `/api/map?project=SBS Sokoto Badagry highway&zoom=9`, fetched once when the Kebbi layer is on (background context only).
- **road_assets clusters** — one `/api/road-assets?project=X&section=Y&<bbox>` per enabled section (`ASSET_SECTIONS` map, `Promise.all`, per-call `.catch` so one transient Supabase SSL blip doesn't sink the others), merged and tagged with their `LayerKey` so a toggle removes them at any zoom (MV-mode cells carry no section). Aggregated timing → `onLoadStats` for `/planning-implementation`'s "Map Load Time" KPI (still works).
- **ArcGIS road-design overlay** — carried over verbatim from HitechMap (`/api/road-design`, Coastal only).
- **Camera:** seeds from `camera ?? readPersistedCamera() ?? REGION_CAMERA[section|national]`; every `idle` writes it back (state + localStorage). **No automatic fitBounds** — the persisted view always wins on load. The only camera moves after init: a `focusRequest`, `/planning-implementation`'s section filter (`REGION_CAMERA`, fires only on a real non-empty change), and a dashboard chainage/category filter change (`filterFitRef`-guarded so it never fights the restored view on a plain load).

**`/api/map` `all=1`** — see the API section above. Two queries merged by id (Coastal set + `.or()` keyword grab of the ~35 other-region rows); note PostgREST's embedded `or()` uses `*` wildcards, not `%` (hit this — `%calabar%` silently matched nothing).

**`/dashboard`** — `HitechMapComponent` → `UnifiedMap`; `handleSelectReport` no longer flips the `project` filter, it just computes `enableLayer` from the report's section/project keyword and calls `setFocusRequest({lat,lng,zoom:16,reportId,enableLayer,popup})`. The dead `focusReport` state is gone.

**`/planning-implementation`** — `RoadAssetsMap` → `UnifiedMap initialSection={activeSection} onLoadStats={setLoadStats}`. `assetsName` on the `PROJECTS` array is now unused (UnifiedMap uses `ASSET_SECTIONS` internally) but left as reference data.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (23 routes). Playwright against `next start` + minted session: `/dashboard` map renders Coastal line + 1,035 report pins + Calabar/Ogun/Kebbi asset clusters with all 6 layer chips, 0 console errors; clicking one of the 12 Calabar report rows pans the map to lat 5.18 / lng 8.31 zoom 16 with the report popup open; setting the map to Kebbi (11.8/4.5/z9) then navigating `/dashboard`→`/planning-implementation`→back restores 11.8/4.5/z9 both times (localStorage); `/planning-implementation` section filter → picking "Section 3 - Calabar" recentres to 4.98/8.33/z11; "Map Load Time" KPI still populates. One transient `/api/road-assets` 500 seen once (Supabase `SSL alert number 51` — network blip, the per-section `.catch` handled it).

### 2026-09-08 (6) — New "Personnel History" section on `/personnel`: click a staff member → merged audit trail

**Files changed:** `src/app/api/personnel-history/route.ts` (new), `src/app/personnel/page.tsx`, `CLAUDE.md`

**What the user asked for:** a new table on the personnel tab — click an employee, see that person's history from `surveycollection_employee_history` and `surveycollection_employee_status_history`.

**What the live data showed (checked before building, via Supabase MCP):**

- Three source tables, all tiny: `surveycollection_employee` (12 rows), `surveycollection_employee_history` (25), `surveycollection_employee_status_history` (7). No perf concern — no RPC, no pagination, one payload, group-by-employee on the client so clicking is instant.
- **The roster is a different population from the `/personnel` bar chart.** That chart ranks `hitech_report_hitechemployee.employee_name` (hundreds of field crew named on activity reports). `surveycollection_employee` is the HR staff directory the main portal's `/api/employees` manages — mostly test accounts + a few real staff, ~zero name overlap (only "Mustapha Ayuba" had meaningful report mentions). So wiring click-to-history onto the existing chart rows would show "no history" for ~everyone. Built a **self-contained roster table** instead, driven by `surveycollection_employee`.
- **A status change is written to both tables** (~0.1s apart) — once into `_status_history`, once into `_history` as a generic `field_name='status'` row. The route dedupes: drop the `_history` copy when a `_status_history` row covers the same employee + old→new value within ~2s. Verified: 32 merged events → 27 after dedup, no real change lost.
- **One orphaned employee** ("Hey Test", id 39): history rows exist but the roster row was deleted. An audit trail that vanishes with the record isn't an audit trail — the route appends `removed: true` roster entries for any `employee_id` that appears only in the history tables, named from the denormalized `employee_name`. They sort to the bottom of the roster and render a "removed from roster" tag.

**`GET /api/personnel-history`** (session-guarded, no params, `Cache-Control: private, max-age=20`): returns `{ employees, history }`. `history` is both tables normalized to one shape (`{ employeeId, kind: 'field'|'status', field, oldValue, newValue, changedBy, changedAt }`), sorted `changedAt` desc. Empty strings → `null`. `.error` on any of the 3 queries → `503` (not a silent empty 200 — the `/progress` "silent zeros" lesson). Deliberately **not** narrowed by the dashboard's category/project/chainage params — neither history table links to an activity report.

**`/personnel` page:** new `PersonnelHistory` component in a `Reveal` below the Supervisors grid — its own `fetch`, independent of the filter bar. Two-pane: a scrollable roster list (name, role, status pill, change count, sorted by most-recent change) and a vertical timeline for the selected employee (defaults to the freshest). Each event: a mono field-label chip, `old → new` (null shown as italic "empty"), `by {changedBy} · {formatted date}`, a dot coloured by kind (status = accent, field = muted) with a connector line. Empty states for staff with no history and for a load failure. Stacks under 760px. Uses the page's existing `Card`/`EmptyState`/`Skel`/`useTheme` primitives — no new shared components.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (24 routes, `/api/personnel-history` registered). Live: `curl` → 401 without cookie, `{employees:13, history:27}` with a minted session (12 roster + 1 removed). Playwright pass (light + dark, 1280–1440px): 13 roster rows, removed employee sorted last with its tag, clicking `ddjzphxwxg` swaps the timeline to its 8 deduped events, "Hey Test" shows the retained-history subheader, `Kwame Asante` (no history) shows the empty state — 0 console errors in either theme.

### 2026-09-08 (5) — `/machines` "Ownership Breakdown": merge to Hitech vs Subcontractor (renting/third-party counts as subcontractor)

**Files changed:** `src/app/api/dashboard/_lib.ts`, `src/app/api/dashboard/route.ts`

**What changed:** the raw `ownership` column on `hitech_report_hitechmachine` has 6 dirty values (`hitech` 9054, `subcontactor` [sic] 975, `Renting - Third party` 375, `HITECH` 17, `Subcontractor` 2, `Renting` 1, plus 40 null) — the donut showed all 6. Per the user, these are two groups: everything Hitech, and everything else (subcontractor + the misspelling + rented/third-party equipment). Added `normalizeOwnershipSeries()` in `_lib.ts` (same shape as `normalizePartySeries`, applied to `byOwnership` in `route.ts` right before `applyUnknownHandling`): `hitech` in the name → **"Hitech"** bucket; `sub[-\s]?cont` / `rent` / `third part` → **"Subcontractor"** bucket; anything else passes through. Labels are forced to those two canonical spellings (unlike the party merge's "dominant raw value" rule) because here the dominant non-Hitech raw value is the `subcontactor` typo. Verified live: donut collapses to `Hitech` 9,071 + `Subcontractor` 1,353 (sum 10,424, matches the old total), the "OWNERSHIP TYPES" KPI goes 6 → 2, and the `No ownership recorded: 40 not shown in ranking` caption (2026-09-08 (4)) is unaffected. `tsc` + `next build` clean; Playwright pass on `/machines`, 0 console errors. **Caveat (same as the party merge):** clicking the merged `Subcontractor` slice filters `ownership=Subcontractor`, an exact match that only catches the 2 literal-"Subcontractor" rows, not the typo'd/renting ones — a `dashboard_filtered_ids` ownership-predicate change would be needed to make that click sweep the whole bucket.

### 2026-09-08 (4) — "Unknown" buckets: recover blank employee names, strip the rest out of the ranked charts (no fabrication)

**Files changed:** `scripts/sql/add_dashboard_unknown_handling.sql` (new) + Supabase migration `add_dashboard_unknown_handling`, `src/app/api/dashboard/_lib.ts`, `src/app/api/dashboard/route.ts`, `src/app/personnel/page.tsx`, `src/app/machines/page.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`

**What the user asked for:** remove the big "Unknown" bar from the person/weather charts (e.g. Engineers Activity was 67% "Unknown"), and "split the data of unknown to the highest and second highest personnel in each table", for every "Unknown" across the project.

**Why it wasn't done as literally asked:** `AskUserQuestion` up front with the real numbers — redistributing 4,499 unattributed engineer activities onto "Believe" (1,151) and "TONY" (563) 3–5×'s two real people's apparent workload with work the records don't tie to them, on a dashboard used to judge who did what, and it's irreversible once screenshotted / used for a staffing or payment call. Same class of integrity call as the 2026-08-01 synthetic-data entries. User picked **"Recover + relabel"**, not the redistribution.

**What "Unknown" actually is (checked live before proposing anything):**
- engineer name: 4,499 / 6,689 rows (67%) have no `engineer_name` and no `engineer_missing_name` — genuinely blank, nothing to recover.
- supervisor name: 266 / 6,720 (4%) genuinely blank.
- weather: 3,676 / 9,776 reports (38%) blank.
- driver: ~3,711 machine mentions blank.
- employee name: only 67 blank, but **53** of those have a real name typed into the ignored `employee_missing_name` free-text column ("person wasn't in the dropdown") — recoverable.
- reporter / project / category / section / status: zero blanks — those charts never had an "Unknown".

**SQL migration `add_dashboard_unknown_handling`** (recreates `dashboard_core` + `dashboard_filtered_ids` in full — a SQL function body can't be partially altered; diff is only the 4 employee expressions, each marked `-- CHANGED`): everywhere those two functions read `employee_name`, it's now `coalesce(nullif(btrim(employee_name),''), employee_missing_name)` — the `byEmployee` group array, the `distinct_tc` 'emp' row, the `emp` CTE `p_employee` predicate, and the `p_employee` EXISTS predicate in `dashboard_filtered_ids`. Net effect: the 53 blank-name employee rows now resolve to their real typed-in name in the ranking and are filterable by it. Nothing else changes; verified `dashboard_core()` still returns the same shape and `byEmployee` no longer contains "Unknown". The `.sql` file is checked in per this repo's convention (no Supabase CLI migrations dir) and was applied to the live project via MCP.

**API route (`_lib.ts` + `route.ts`)** — after the RPC returns, and after `normalizePartySeries` (see the 2026-09-08 (3) entry): `applyUnknownHandling(core)` pulls any entry literally named `"Unknown"` (what `_dash_group` emits for a blank raw value) out of `byEngineer`, `bySupervisor`, `byEmployee`, `byMachine`, `byDriver`, `byOwnership`, `byWeather`, `byEmployeeRole`, `byEngineerParty`, `bySupervisorParty`. The cleaned series replace the originals (so the frontend ranks and computes % over named entities only), and the removed counts are attached as a new `unattributed: { byEngineer: 4499, byWeather: 3676, byDriver: 3711, … }` map (non-zero entries only). Recomputes correctly per active filter (checked `?category=Earthworks` → `byEngineer` unattributed 1,282). `byCategory`/`byProject`/`byStatus` are deliberately **not** in the list — no blanks there, and a fabricated-looking "Unknown status" split would be worse than leaving it.

**Frontend disclosure** — the gap is still shown, just not fabricated onto anyone or counted in the ranks:
- `personnel/page.tsx`, `machines/page.tsx`: `Card` gained an optional `note` prop (muted mono line under the chart body); a local `unattributedNote(data, key, subject)` helper renders `"No engineer recorded: 4,499 not shown in ranking"` under Engineers/Supervisors/Employees-by-role and Machines/Ownership/Drivers when `unattributed[key] > 0`.
- `dashboard/page.tsx`: the Weather Conditions card passes the same text through its existing `sub` prop.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (23 routes). Live Playwright pass (minted session): `/personnel` — Engineers chart now leads with Believe (53% of recorded), no "Unknown" bar anywhere on the page, the three footnotes render (`No engineer recorded: 4,499` / `No supervisor recorded: 266` / `No role recorded: 1`), party donuts still show the merged 2 slices, 0 console errors. `/dashboard` — Weather is now Sunny 95% of recorded with `No weather recorded: 3,676 not shown in ranking` under the title, 0 console errors.

### 2026-09-08 (3) — `/personnel` "Engineers by Party" / "Supervisors by Party": merge dirty label variants

**Files changed:** `src/app/api/dashboard/_lib.ts`, `src/app/api/dashboard/route.ts`

**What changed:** the raw `party` column on `hitech_report_hitechengineer` / `hitechsupervisor` has variants that are really only two parties — `Hitech employees` / `HITECH employees`, and `Sub-contractor` / `Sub-contactor` (misspelled). SQL `_titlecase()` only fixes the first letter of each word, so those rendered as 4 separate donut slices. Added `normalizePartySeries()` in `_lib.ts` (buckets any label containing `hitech` → one slice, any `sub-cont…` → one slice, sums the counts, labels each bucket with its highest-count raw value so click-to-filter still matches the bulk of the rows), applied to `byEngineerParty` / `bySupervisorParty` in `route.ts` before the response is cached. Verified against live data: supervisors collapse to `Hitech Employees` (6,240) + `Sub-contractor` (480); engineers to `Hitech Employees` (6,489) + `Sub-contactor` (200). `tsc` + `next build` clean. **Caveat:** clicking the merged `Sub-contractor` slice filters by that exact string, so the 63 typo'd `Sub-contactor` supervisor rows aren't included in that filtered view (they were their own slice before) — a `dashboard_filtered_ids` party-predicate tweak would be needed to sweep the misspelling in too. Real root cause is the portal writing the dirty values; normalizing at read time here.

### 2026-09-08 (2) — Shell "premium" pass: new type system, genuinely fixed rail, full-bleed content, and a latent font bug fixed

**Files changed:** `src/app/globals.css`, `src/app/layout.tsx`, `src/components/AppShell.tsx` (new), `src/components/SideNav.tsx`, `src/components/DashHeader.tsx`, `src/lib/theme.tsx`, `src/lib/theme-constants.ts`, `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`, plus the container div + Suspense-fallback of `machines`/`personnel`/`planning-implementation`/`road-assets-coverage`/`progress` pages.

**What the user asked for (4 points):** (1) the type felt "mundane" — wanted distinct professional faces "that match their station"; (2) the sidebar should be a true top-to-bottom rail that does **not** move with page scroll; (3) main content should fill the screen edge-to-edge like a supplied reference dashboard; (4) premium/liven it up, using the `ui-ux-pro-max` + `shadcn-ui` skills. Ran `ui-ux-pro-max --design-system` (→ "Data-Dense Dashboard" style, mono+sans pairing direction). `shadcn-ui` in this catalogue is an upstream stub with no runnable assets, and this codebase has **no** shadcn/Tailwind-component layer (every component is inline-styled against the `useTheme()` token object) — so shadcn *conventions* were adopted (compound `Card`, muted single-accent palette, consistent radius, ring focus, sidebar active-accent) rather than installing the library, which would have been a 6-page rewrite.

**Typography — 3 distinct roles (was: Geist everywhere):**
- `--font-display` / `--font-loader` → **Space Grotesk** (headings, card titles via a new global `h1..h5` rule, and every big KPI number — a technical drafting-table character).
- `--font-body` / `--font-dm-sans` → **Fira Sans** (body, labels, tables — the skill's data-dashboard pick, humanist but precise at small sizes).
- `--font-mono` / `--font-dm-mono` → **JetBrains Mono** (chainages, codes, mono labels).
- `next/font/google` faces declared in `layout.tsx` with explicit weights; variable names `--font-space-grotesk` / `--font-fira-sans` / `--font-jetbrains-mono`.
- **Latent bug fixed along the way:** the semantic `--font-*` aliases were declared on `:root`, but `next/font` injects its `--font-*` variables onto `<body>` (via a className). So `var(--font-fira-sans)` was **undefined at `:root` scope**, which made the whole `--font-body` declaration invalid-at-computed-value-time — the site was silently rendering a serif (`Times New Roman`) fallback, not Geist, and had been since the Geist switch (2026-09-07). Fixed by moving the semantic alias block from `:root` into `body {}`, where the source vars are in scope. Verified in-browser: `getComputedStyle(document.body).fontFamily` now resolves to Fira Sans, `<h2>`/`<h3>` to Space Grotesk, and the woff2 faces report `loaded`.

**Shell layout — `src/components/AppShell.tsx` (new client component):** `layout.tsx` (a server component) can't read `useSidebar()`, so the frame moved into `AppShell`: it renders `<SideNav/>` + a content column whose `margin-left` equals the current rail width (`SIDEBAR_WIDTH` / `SIDEBAR_WIDTH_COLLAPSED`, new constants in `theme-constants.ts`, imported by both sides so they can't drift; `0` on `/login` and on mobile). `SideNav` is now **`position: fixed` on desktop too** (was `position: sticky` — which is fragile under the `html,body { overflow-x: hidden }` in globals, and was the likely cause of the "it scrolls with the page" report). Rail is `top:0; bottom:0; height:100vh`, out of flow entirely. Rail background went `#fbfcfd`→`#ffffff` (light) / `#0d1319`→`#0c1218` (dark) + a soft right-edge shadow so it reads as a panel, not empty space (the user's screenshot showed it blending into the page). Active nav item gained an `inset 3px 0` accent bar + trailing dot + a 1px icon nudge on hover.

**Full-bleed content:** every page's `<div style={{ padding:'24px', maxWidth:1240, margin:'0 auto' }}>` (and the matching Suspense-fallback wrapper) → `padding:'28px 36px', width:'100%'` — no centered 1240px column anymore. Dashboard adds a `@media (max-width:1200px)` step-down to `24px`.

**Liveliness / polish (dashboard + shared tokens):**
- `theme.tsx` shadow tokens `card`/`cardLg`/`panel`/`panelLg`/`raised*` deepened slightly in both themes (the old light `card` was `0 1px 2px /.06` — invisible on `#f5f7f9`). No interface/field changes.
- `DashHeader` sticky bar: `background` → `${D.panel}f2` + `backdrop-filter: saturate(180%) blur(8px)` + `boxShadow: SH.card`, padding `0 12px`→`0 16px`.
- Dashboard `Card`: radius 10→12, `className="ui-card"` + a `.ui-card:hover` rule (deeper shadow, `${D.amber}44` border, `translateY(-1px)`), title 13.5→14 / `-0.02em`.
- Dashboard "Overview" mini-grid: mono-uppercase micro labels, numbers 20→27 in Space Grotesk, vertical divider between cells (dropped on wrap via media rules), and the Completion cell gained an inline green progress bar. Greeting `<h2>` 18→22.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean (22 pages). Playwright pass against a fresh `next start` + minted session, `/dashboard` in light & dark at 1600px and `/dashboard` + `/progress` at mobile 390px — zero console/page errors; DOM probes confirm the rail is `position:fixed` at `(0,0)`, `256px` wide, full viewport height, the content column starts at `x=264` (right of the rail), no horizontal scroll, and the drawer sits off-canvas at `-288` on mobile with the content reclaiming full width. **One process-hygiene note for next time:** a zombie `next start` from an earlier attempt kept serving a stale `.next` (500s on the CSS chunk, fonts "missing") and briefly looked like a real regression — `rm -rf .next` + one clean build/start resolved it; kill prior servers by PID (`netstat -ano | grep :3000`) before restarting.

**Not done:** the other 5 pages got the shell + font + full-width treatment for free but their *page-body* chrome (filter bars, some chart spacing) wasn't re-polished this pass; `HitechMap`/`RoadAssetsMap` still carry their own hardcoded styling and amber legend (same open item as the 2026-09-07 entries).

### 2026-09-08 — Dashboard loading-speed overhaul (DB load, API round trips, skeleton gating)

**Files changed:** `src/app/api/dashboard/route.ts` (rewritten), `src/app/api/dashboard/_lib.ts` (new), `src/app/api/dashboard/extra/route.ts` (new), `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx` (`loadData`), `scripts/sql/add_dashboard_rpcs.sql` (new); Supabase migrations `drop_streetlights_infra_and_unused_road_assets_indexes`, `add_dashboard_rpcs` (+ `fix_dashboard_rpcs_setof_alias`, `add_dashboard_titlecase_helper`, `optimize_dashboard_core_titlecase`, `dashboard_extra_drop_unused_mappoints`); `cron.job` edits (no migration).

**Why:** user reported the dashboard skeleton showing for far too long. Diagnostics found the cause was almost entirely *outside* the dashboard code — three layers, fixed in order of impact.

**Root cause (measured, not assumed):** the Supabase instance is a Micro tier (`shared_buffers` 256 MB, `work_mem` 3.5 MB, `effective_cache_size` 768 MB, 2 parallel workers, `service_role` has no `statement_timeout` so slow queries crawl rather than erroring). It held two "scale-test" tables — `road_assets` (3.43 GB / 7.27 M rows) and `streetlights` (1.68 GB / 3 M rows) — and **three `pg_cron` jobs full-scanned them every 10–15 min around the clock**: `refresh_streetlights_grid_mv` + `refresh_streetlights_summary_cache` (both `*/10`, ~5–37 s and ~14–27 s each, firing on the same tick so they overlap) and `refresh_road_assets_summary_cache` (`*/15`, avg **46 s** — a `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY` plus four full `GROUP BY` scans of 7.27 M rows). Measured DB cache-hit ratio was **69%** (healthy is >99%): the ~35 MB dashboard working set was being evicted on a loop. `pg_stat_statements` showed the dashboard's own queries had fine medians (5–135 ms) but p95/max of 0.5–2 s each — and the old route fired ~8 parallel query *branches*, several of them multi-page `fetchAll` waterfalls (10–16 sequential PostgREST round trips), so the `Promise.all` was gated by whichever branch hit the slow tail.

**P0 — stop the background load (cron + DB only, no deploy):**
- `cron.unschedule('refresh_streetlights_grid_mv')` and `('refresh_streetlights_summary_cache')` — the `/streetlights` page/route/component were deleted 2026-09-07, these caches feed nothing.
- `refresh_road_assets_summary_cache` rescheduled `*/15` → `0 3 * * *` (nightly). It's static survey data; 15-minute refresh was never warranted.
- `vacuum (analyze) hitech_report_hitechreport` (was 18.9% dead rows, last autovacuum 2026-05-25) + lowered `autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor` to 0.02 on it and the machine/employee join tables.

**P3 — reclaim memory (migration `drop_streetlights_infra_and_unused_road_assets_indexes`):**
- Dropped the whole streetlights data infrastructure — `streetlights` table, `streetlights_grid_mv`, `streetlights_section_stats`, `streetlights_summary_cache`, `streetlight_image_variants`, and the `streetlights_cluster`/`streetlights_clusters`/`streetlights_detail`/`streetlights_refresh_summary_cache`/`streetlights_set_geom` functions. (The 2026-09-07 entry deliberately left these; user confirmed dropping them now. `RoadAssetsMap.tsx` / `src/app/api/road-assets/route.ts` still carry lineage *comments* mentioning streetlights — harmless, don't reference live objects.)
- Dropped 4 `road_assets` indexes with zero recorded scans: `road_assets_geom_idx` (297 MB GIST), `road_assets_extra_gin_idx` (113 MB GIN on the `extra` jsonb), `idx_road_assets_section`, `idx_road_assets_project` (49 MB each — superseded by the composite `road_assets_section_entity_*` / `road_assets_project_section_idx`). The lat/lon btree and composites that `/api/road-assets` actually uses are untouched.
- Net: DB size ~5.4 GB → ~3.7 GB.

**P1 — collapse the API to one query per endpoint (migration `add_dashboard_rpcs`, `scripts/sql/add_dashboard_rpcs.sql`):**
- Three Postgres functions replace the route's ~6 `fetchAll()` waterfalls + in-JS reduce: `dashboard_filtered_ids(...)` (the shared filter predicate — real columns + the machine/employee/engineer/supervisor/ownership/driver/role/party filters as `EXISTS` subqueries, all case-insensitive `lower(btrim())` matches, same semantics as the old in-memory `matchReportIds`), `dashboard_core(...)` (KPIs + every `by*` breakdown + the 4 `*Summary` distinct-counts + `filterOptions` → one `jsonb`), `dashboard_extra(...)` (`mediaItems` + `activityCalendar` + `recentReports` → one `jsonb`). Same one-call/one-scan discipline as the `progress_*` RPCs.
- `_titlecase()` reproduces the old route's `toTitleCase()` exactly (upper-case first letter of each space-separated word, leave the rest — so `"GPS"` stays `"GPS"`, `"gps"` → `"Gps"`; `initcap()` was rejected because it also lowercases, changing `"GPS"`→`"Gps"`, `"SBS Sokoto..."`→`"Sbs Sokoto..."`, and all-caps names). It's ~40× a C builtin, so every call site collapses values to their DISTINCT set before applying it — this took `dashboard_core()` from 2.2 s to ~0.4–0.65 s on the *fully unfiltered* table (worst case; filtered is faster).
- `dashboard_extra` returns `mapPoints: []` — nothing consumes it (`HitechMap` fetches `/api/map` itself), so the ~3.8k-row array build was dropped.
- `src/app/api/dashboard/route.ts` is now: session check → check a 30 s in-process TTL cache (keyed by querystring; the payload is identical for every user) → one `rpcWithRetry('dashboard_core', …)` (retry-once on `.error`, `503` if it still fails — never a silent zero-filled body, same lesson as the 2026-07-22 `/progress` fix) → spread the jsonb + attach `activeFilters`. `GET /api/dashboard/extra` mirrors it for `dashboard_extra`. Shared helpers (`dashboardRpcArgs`, `activeFiltersFrom`, `rpcWithRetry`, `makeTtlCache`, the supabase client) live in `src/app/api/dashboard/_lib.ts` so the extra route imports them without re-exporting HTTP handlers. Both send `Cache-Control: private, max-age=20, stale-while-revalidate=60`.

**P2 — unblock the skeleton (`dashboard/page.tsx` `loadData`):**
- `loadData` now fires `/api/dashboard` and `/api/dashboard/extra` **in parallel**. Core resolves → `setData({ ...EMPTY_HEAVY, ...core })` (heavy fields seeded as `[]`) and the skeleton clears immediately on KPIs + charts + filter options. Extra resolves → merged in (`setData(prev => ({ ...prev, ...x }))`), filling the calendar, media gallery and recent-reports feed a beat later. A `pendingExtraRef` handles the rare case where extra (cache hit) resolves before core, and both fetches are gated on `requestIdRef` so a stale response can't clobber a newer filter change. `DashData` shape is unchanged.

**Label normalization note:** group-key casing is now produced by SQL `_titlecase()` instead of the JS `toTitleCase()`. Verified equivalent on the real data — e.g. `machine=GPS` still returns `byMachine: [{name:"GPS", count:3325}]` (matches the 2026-08-11 entry), unfiltered top machine is still `"Gps"` (separate raw bucket), `distinctMachines` for a specific machine is still `1`.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next build` clean (23 routes, `/api/dashboard/extra` registered). RPCs exercised against live data — `dashboard_core()` summary (`totalReports 9776`, `completionRate 8`), `byStatus`/`byCategory`/`byMachine` shapes, filter paths (`category=Earthworks` → 1798, `machine=GPS` → 3325 / distinct 1), `dashboard_extra()` (`recentReports` field-for-field matches the old `recentReports` select, 12 rows / 300 under `search`, `mediaItems` 600, `activityCalendar` 606). `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`: core 365 ms filtered / 471–657 ms unfiltered, extra ~470 ms — vs. the old multi-second-under-cache-pressure path. **Not yet done:** a live browser/Playwright pass on `/dashboard` (build + API-level checks are real but stop short of confirming the two-stage render in the actual UI).

### 2026-09-07 — Design system rebuild, Phase 2: the other 5 pages converted to the achromatic shell kit

**Files changed:** `src/app/progress/page.tsx`, `src/app/machines/page.tsx`, `src/app/personnel/page.tsx`, `src/app/planning-implementation/page.tsx`, `src/app/road-assets-coverage/page.tsx`

**What changed** — the Phase-1 pass (below) converted the shell + `/dashboard`; this pass applies the same treatment to every remaining page. Each was rewritten presentationally while keeping **all** data logic, hooks, handlers, fetch flows, chart math, and the type interfaces byte-identical — this is a reskin, not a refactor.

- **Shared primitives, per page**: the skeuomorphic `Panel` (pinging amber dot + mono badge chip on `D.bg`) → flat `Card` (`D.panel` surface, `1px` hairline, `10px` radius, `SH.card` whisper shadow, plain `<h3>` + optional `.sub`, no chrome). `KPICard`/`StatCard` (corner radial glow, `linear-gradient` accent bar, `var(--font-loader)` colour-on-colour number, hover glow) → flat card: small `D.panel2` icon chip, number in `D.text` (semantic colour only where it means something — `green` for completion/implemented, `red` for delayed/gaps), Geist 600 + `tabular-nums`. `Reveal` simplified (drop the scale-in). Skeleton shimmer is grey (`D.panel2`), not amber.
- **Sub-headers / page heads**: `/progress` and `/road-assets-coverage` had sticky ticker sub-headers with a Bebas-glow wordmark and pill tabs carrying `SH.glowAmber` — replaced with a quiet `<h2>` + muted `<p>` (or a compact sticky bar for `/progress`, now at `top: 3.5rem` to sit under the new 56px top bar, was `top: 52`) and **underline tabs** (`border-bottom: 2px solid accent` on the active tab, muted text otherwise) matching the artifact. `/machines` + `/personnel` + `/planning-implementation` lost their `var(--font-loader)` all-caps titles for the same `<h2>`/`<p>` head.
- **Charts → single-hue + semantic-only**: every `HBarChart` → top-3 bars in the sky accent, the rest `D.muted`, shimmer/glow removed, `rgba(255,255,255,0.04)` tracks → `D.panel2`. `DonutChart` → a single-hue ramp (`[accent, accent+c8, accent+96, accent+64, muted, muted+b0, muted+80]`) replacing the 7-colour `CAT_COLORS`. `/progress`: `ProgressCurve` lost its 3-stop gradient + line-glow (flat accent line, `${accent}14` area); `GanttChart`'s 5-shade amber `COLORS` array → one flat accent; `DelayDonut` → green/red (was green/amber) with glows removed; `MonthlyProgressTable`'s `SIDE_COLOR` RHS `blue`→`muted`, pending cells amber→`muted`. `/road-assets-coverage`: `Gauge`/`TimelineChart`/`PlannedActualBars`/`EntityGanttChart` all de-glowed and re-tokened, SVG tooltips `#0a0a0c`→`D.panel`+`D.border`; `CompletionHistogram` keeps its red/amber/green bands (those *are* the data — completion ranges); `EntityGanttChart` keeps status colours (Completed green / Delayed red / In Progress accent / Not Started grey — also the data); LHS/RHS/Median in `EntityCompletionRow` → `accent`/`muted`/`green` (was `amber`/`blue`/`green`, and `blue` now collapses onto `accent`).
- **Tables**: `D.amber` headers → `D.muted` on a `D.panel2` band; `rgba(255,255,255,0.03)` row borders → `D.border`; `.tbl-row` zebra now `${D.panel2}66`, hover `${D.amber}12` (both interpolated from tokens so they work in light mode — the hardcoded `rgba(255,255,255,…)` values were invisible-on-white before). Status/rate chips unified into a small `Pill` (`ok`/`accent`/`crit`/`mut`). Pagers + segmented toggles factored into shared `Pager`/`Seg` helpers, flat-styled.
- **Filter bars**: the `linear-gradient` amber "Apply" buttons → flat accent with **white** text (the sky accent is dark enough that near-black text failed contrast); gradient/glow "Clear" → ghost outline; the coloured applied-filter chips → `Pill kind="mut"`; `D.bg` select backgrounds → `D.panel2`; every page's local `<style>` block had its amber scrollbar override, `btn-ghost`/`seg-btn` glow-hover rules, and hardcoded `rgba(255,255,255,…)` swapped for token interpolation or removed (scrollbars are handled globally now).
- **Not touched**: `HitechMap` / `RoadAssetsMap` still carry their own hardcoded Google-Maps styling and amber cluster/legend colours — same open item as Phase 1, the one remaining multi-hue spot on `/dashboard` and `/planning-implementation`.
- **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean after each page; `next build` clean (all 21 routes). Playwright pass (minted session) across all 5 pages in **both** light and dark at 1440px, plus tab switches on `/progress` (Overview→Planning→BOQ→Activity Reports) and `/road-assets-coverage` (Overview→Timeline→By Chainage) — **zero** console/page errors anywhere; screenshots confirm the achromatic + single-accent treatment renders correctly in both themes with real data (`/personnel`, `/planning-implementation`, `/road-assets-coverage` timeline all verified visually with data loaded).

**Why:** Direct "go and do the rest" after Phase 1 shipped. Each page was a full presentational rewrite rather than hundreds of scattered style edits — the same approach that worked for `/dashboard`, and lower-risk than surgically hunting every `${D.amber}20` / `rgba(255,255,255,…)` / glow across ~4,000 lines. Data paths were preserved verbatim so behaviour is unchanged; only the pixels moved.

### 2026-09-07 — Design system rebuild, Phase 1: achromatic "shell kit" — new theme, sidebar, top bar, and a fully-restyled `/dashboard`

**Files changed:** `src/lib/theme.tsx`, `src/lib/theme-constants.ts`, `src/lib/sidebar.tsx` (new), `src/app/globals.css`, `src/app/layout.tsx`, `src/components/SideNav.tsx`, `src/components/DashHeader.tsx`, `src/components/ThemeToggle.tsx`, `src/app/login/page.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`

**What changed** — user asked to rebuild the dashboard against an external reference artifact (the "Iconic Freeze Shell Kit"): minimal/achromatic colour, three-state theme, grouped collapsible sidebar, compact top bar. Confirmed scope via `AskUserQuestion` (all four recommended options): shell + `/dashboard` first (other 5 pages roll out in a Phase 2), full grouped sidebar (not a flat list), restyle charts to the minimal palette, adopt Geist / Geist Mono.
